Pattern parsing from comment line:
```go
func parseEmbedPatterns(line string) ([]string, error) {
    // line is like "//go:embed image/* template/*"
    line = strings.TrimSpace(strings.TrimPrefix(line, "//"))
    if !strings.HasPrefix(line, "go:embed") { return nil, nil }
    rest := strings.TrimSpace(line[len("go:embed"):])
    if rest == "" { return nil, errors.New("go:embed: empty pattern") }
    // tokenize, handling quoted patterns
}
```

Wait, we should only treat `//go:embed` (exact) and not `//go:embedfoo`. Check that after "go:embed" there's whitespace or end. Also comments can be
`/* go:embed ... */`? Go only supports line comments for embed. So check prefix "//go:embed" or maybe there's leading whitespace before //.
`cg.List[i].Text` includes the comment markers. For a line `//go:embed foo`, Text is "//go:embed foo". For block comments we won't support.

Let me write:
```go
func embedPatterns(cg *ast.CommentGroup) ([]string, error) {
    if cg == nil { return nil, nil }
    var patterns []string
    for _, c := range cg.List {
        txt := c.Text
        if len(txt) < len("//go:embed") || txt[:len("//go:embed")] != "//go:embed" {
            continue
        }
        // ensure it's exactly "go:embed" not "go:embedX"
        rest := txt[len("//go:embed"):]
        if rest != "" && rest[0] != ' ' && rest[0] != '\t' {
            continue
        }
        ps, err := parseEmbedPatterns(rest)
        ...
        patterns = append(patterns, ps...)
    }
    return patterns, nil
}
```

Actually `//go:embed` with no space after is valid? In Go, `//go:embedfoo` is not a directive; the directive is `//go:embed` followed by space. We'll
require rest empty or starts with whitespace.

parseEmbedPatterns tokenizer:
```go
func parseEmbedPatterns(s string) ([]string, error) {
    s = strings.TrimSpace(s)
    var patterns []string
    for len(s) > 0 {
        switch s[0] {
        case '"', '`':
            // quoted string
            end := 1
            for end < len(s) {
                if s[0] == '"' && s[end] == '\\' {
                    end += 2
                    continue
                }
                if s[end] == s[0] {
                    break
                }
                end++
            }
            if end >= len(s) {
                return nil, errors.New("unterminated quoted pattern")
            }
            q := s[:end+1]
            p, err := strconv.Unquote(q)
            if err != nil { return nil, err }
            patterns = append(patterns, p)
            s = strings.TrimSpace(s[end+1:])
        default:
            end := strings.IndexAny(s, " \t\r\n")
            if end < 0 { end = len(s) }
            patterns = append(patterns, s[:end])
            s = strings.TrimSpace(s[end:])
        }
    }
    return patterns, nil
}
```

This is okay. But if there's a quoted pattern followed immediately by another token without space, Go would probably error, but we'll not worry.

Now, resolving patterns against a directory using fs.FS.

I need a function to collect all entries under dir:
```go
func collectEntries(fsys fs.FS, dir string) (map[string]bool /*dirs*/, map[string]bool /*files*/, error)
```
Where keys are paths relative to dir. Since we walk from dir, `dir` is the absolute-ish path used to open. `fs.ReadDir(fsys, dir)` gives entries. For
each entry, rel := path.Join(rel, e.Name()) starting from "". For subdirectories, recurse.

Note: if dir is "." (for Eval), fs.ReadDir(fsys, ".") works with realFS? os.Open(".") works. With MapFS, fs.ReadDir(MapFS, ".") works. Good.

Then for each pattern:
```go
func (interp *Interpreter) resolveEmbed(dir string, patterns []string) (*embedData, error) {
    dirs, files, err := collectEntries(interp.opt.filesystem, dir)
    ...
    data := &embedData{dir: dir, patterns: patterns, files: map[string][]byte{}}
    matchedAny := false
    for _, pat := range patterns {
        all, p := splitAll(pat)
        if err := validatePattern(p); err != nil { return nil, err }
        // Check dirs
        for d := range dirs {
            if path.Match(p, d) == nil {
                // include tree under d
                for f := range files {
                    if f == d || strings.HasPrefix(f, d+"/") {
                        if all || visible(f) {
                            if err := addFile(data, f); err != nil { return nil, err }
                            matchedAny = true
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        // Check files
        for f := range files {
            if path.Match(p, f) == nil {
                if all || visible(f) {
                    addFile...
                    matchedAny = true
                }
            }
        }
    }
    if !matchedAny {
        return nil, fmt.Errorf("pattern %s: no matching files found", strings.Join(patterns, " "))
    }
    return data, nil
}
```

Wait, but this approach has a problem: If a pattern matches a directory but there are no visible files under it (e.g., all hidden), matchedAny should
be true because the pattern matched a directory? Go says "each pattern must match at least one file or non-empty directory." So matching a directory
(even if empty or all hidden?) — actually if directory matches but all files hidden, then no files are embedded and it might be an error. Go's rule:
"Matches for empty directories are ignored. After that, each pattern ... must match at least one file or non-empty directory." So if a dir matches but
contains no visible files, the match is ignored and may cause "no matching files" error. Our matchedAny should be set only if we actually include at
least one file. But if dir matched but no files included, we should NOT count it. Let's refine: matchedAny = true when at least one file added for
that pattern. If a pattern matches a directory that has no visible files, it doesn't count, and if no other pattern matches, error.

Actually, we need per-pattern tracking to report which pattern failed. Let's track `matchedThisPattern`. If after all patterns, total files == 0 →
error "no matching files found". But to give better error, track per pattern: after processing each pattern, if it matched nothing (no file included),
record error and return. However, multiple patterns can each match; we need each to match at least one. Let's implement per-pattern check: after
processing pattern, if `count` for this pattern == 0 → return error.

But wait, `all:` patterns: same logic.

Also, dedupe: same file matched by multiple patterns is fine.

Now `visible(path)`: return true if no component starts with . or _. Components split by "/". For the root-level file `hello.txt`, components =
["hello.txt"], visible true. For `.hidden`, false. For `dir/.hidden`, false. For `dir/sub/file`, all visible.

```go
func visible(p string) bool {
    for _, part := range strings.Split(p, "/") {
        if strings.HasPrefix(part, ".") || strings.HasPrefix(part, "_") {
            return false
        }
    }
    return true
}
```

Now pattern validation:
- Reject if p == "".
- Reject if strings.HasPrefix(p, "/") or strings.HasSuffix(p, "/").
- Reject if contains "\\".
- Split by "/", reject "." or ".." or "" elements.
- Also reject p == "." or p == "..".
- path.Match(p, "dummy") to validate glob syntax. But path.Match with a valid pattern returns (bool, error); error only for malformed. We can call
path.Match(p, "x") and check err.

Also, Go's embed rejects patterns containing ":" and "*" in filename? Actually "*" is part of glob. The restriction is about matched file names
containing special punctuation. We won't enforce that.

Let me also handle the case where `dir` doesn't exist. fs.ReadDir returns error; we return it.

Now `addFile` reads content:
```go
func (data *embedData) addFile(fsys fs.FS, dir, rel string) error {
    if _, ok := data.files[rel]; ok { return nil }
    full := path.Join(dir, rel)
    b, err := fs.ReadFile(fsys, full)
    if err != nil { return err }
    data.files[rel] = b
    return nil
}
```
If dir == "." and rel == "hello.txt", full = "hello.txt". Good.

Now collectEntries:
```go
func collectEntries(fsys fs.FS, dir string) (map[string]bool, map[string]bool, error) {
    dirs := map[string]bool{}
    files := map[string]bool{}
    var walk func(string, string) error
    walk = func(root, rel string) error {
        entries, err := fs.ReadDir(fsys, root)
        if err != nil { return err }
        for _, e := range entries {
            childRel := e.Name()
            if rel != "" { childRel = path.Join(rel, e.Name()) }
            childRoot := path.Join(root, e.Name())
            if e.IsDir() {
                dirs[childRel] = true
                if err := walk(childRoot, childRel); err != nil { return err }
            } else {
                files[childRel] = true
            }
        }
        return nil
    }
    if err := walk(dir, ""); err != nil { return nil, nil, err }
    return dirs, files, nil
}
```

Wait, for fs.ReadDir on MapFS, directory entries are implicit. If dir="." and MapFS has "hello.txt", ReadDir(".") returns hello.txt. Good. For subdir
"sub", MapFS with "sub/a.txt" — ReadDir("sub") returns a.txt. Good.

One issue: If `dir` is relative like "." and root is "main.go", path.Join(".", "main.go") = "main.go". Good.

Now, `path.Match(p, d)` for a dir path like "subdir" returns match if pattern "subdir" or "sub*" etc.

Now, one important Go embed subtlety: A pattern like `*.txt` should match files in the base directory only. Since our rel paths for top-level files
are "hello.txt", path.Match("*.txt","hello.txt") = true. It won't match "sub/hello.txt" because * doesn't cross "/". Good.

A pattern like `dir/*` matches "dir/a.txt" and "dir/sub" (dir). Good.

Now, pattern `dir` matches dir "dir", includes tree. Good.

Now, what about pattern `dir/` (trailing slash)? We reject.

Now let's consider the `all:` prefix and pattern matching. In Go, `all:image/*` means include hidden files in the image subtree. Our `all` flag
controls visibility for all matches under that pattern. Good.

type astNode struct {
	node *node
	ast  ast.Node
}

type nodestack []astNode

func (s *nodestack) push(n *node, a ast.Node) {
	*s = append(*s, astNode{n, a})
}

func (s *nodestack) pop() astNode {
	l := len(*s) - 1
	res := (*s)[l]
	*s = (*s)[:l]
	return res
}

func (s *nodestack) top() astNode {
	l := len(*s)
	if l > 0 {
		return (*s)[l-1]
	}
	return astNode{}
}

package interp

import (
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"go/ast"
	"io"
	"io/fs"
	"path"
	"reflect"
	"sort"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
	"time"
)

// embedData stores the resolved files of a //go:embed directive.
type embedData struct {
	dir      string
	patterns []string
	files    map[string][]byte
}

// embedPatterns extracts the //go:embed directives from a comment group.
// It returns the list of patterns found in all the embed lines.
func embedPatterns(cg *ast.CommentGroup) ([]string, error) {
	if cg == nil {
		return nil, nil
	}
	var patterns []string
	for _, c := range cg.List {
		text := c.Text
		if len(text) < len("//go:embed") || text[:len("//go:embed")] != "//go:embed" {
			continue
		}
		rest := text[len("//go:embed"):]
		if rest != "" && rest[0] != ' ' && rest[0] != '\t' {
			// Not a go:embed directive, just a comment starting with go:embed.
			continue
		}
		ps, err := parseEmbedPatterns(rest)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
		patterns = append(patterns, ps...)
	}
	return patterns, nil
}

// parseEmbedPatterns splits a go:embed line into its patterns. Quoted
// patterns are supported as in the Go specification.
func parseEmbedPatterns(s string) ([]string, error) {
	s = strings.TrimSpace(s)
	if s == "" {
		return nil, errors.New("go:embed: empty pattern")
	}
	var patterns []string
	for len(s) > 0 {
		switch s[0] {
		case '"', '`':
			// Find the end of the quoted string.
			end := 1
			for end < len(s) {
				if s[0] == '"' && s[end] == '\\' {
					end += 2
					continue
				}
				if s[end] == s[0] {
					break
				}
				end++
			}
			if end >= len(s) {
				return nil, errors.New("go:embed: unterminated quoted pattern")
			}
			p, err := strconv.Unquote(s[:end+1])
			if err != nil {
				return nil, err
			}
			patterns = append(patterns, p)
			s = strings.TrimSpace(s[end+1:])
		default:
			end := strings.IndexAny(s, " \t\r\n")
			if end < 0 {
				end = len(s)
			}
			patterns = append(patterns, s[:end])
			s = strings.TrimSpace(s[end:])
		}
	}
	return patterns, nil
}

// splitAll returns the pattern without the "all:" prefix and a boolean
// indicating whether the prefix was present.
func splitAll(pattern string) (string, bool) {
	if strings.HasPrefix(pattern, "all:") {
		return pattern[4:], true
	}
	return pattern, false
}

// validateEmbedPattern checks that a pattern is valid for use in a
// //go:embed directive.
func validateEmbedPattern(pattern string) error {
	if pattern == "" {
		return errors.New("go:embed: empty pattern")
	}
	if pattern == "." || pattern == ".." {
		return fmt.Errorf("go:embed: invalid pattern %q", pattern)
	}
	if strings.HasPrefix(pattern, "/") || strings.HasSuffix(pattern, "/") {
		return fmt.Errorf("go:embed: invalid pattern %q", pattern)
	}
	if strings.Contains(pattern, "\\") {
		return fmt.Errorf("go:embed: invalid pattern %q", pattern)
	}
	for _, part := range strings.Split(pattern, "/") {
		if part == "" || part == "." || part == ".." {
			return fmt.Errorf("go:embed: invalid pattern %q", pattern)
		}
	}
	if _, err := path.Match(pattern, "dummy"); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("go:embed: invalid pattern %q: %v", pattern, err)
	}
	return nil
}

// visible reports whether a relative path contains no hidden component
// (a component beginning with '.' or '_').
func visible(name string) bool {
	for _, part := range strings.Split(name, "/") {
		if strings.HasPrefix(part, ".") || strings.HasPrefix(part, "_") {
			return false
		}
	}
	return true
}

// collectEmbedEntries walks the directory dir and returns the relative paths
// of all directories and regular files it contains.
func collectEmbedEntries(fsys fs.FS, dir string) (dirs, files map[string]bool, err error) {
	dirs = map[string]bool{}
	files = map[string]bool{}
	var walk func(root, rel string) error
	walk = func(root, rel string) error {
		entries, err := fs.ReadDir(fsys, root)
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		for _, e := range entries {
			childRel := e.Name()
			if rel != "" {
				childRel = path.Join(rel, e.Name())
			}
			childRoot := path.Join(root, e.Name())
			if e.IsDir() {
				dirs[childRel] = true
				if err := walk(childRoot, childRel); err != nil {
					return err
				}
			} else {
				files[childRel] = true
			}
		}
		return nil
	}
	if err := walk(dir, ""); err != nil {
		return nil, nil, err
	}
	return dirs, files, nil
}

// resolveEmbed resolves the patterns against the source directory dir and
// reads the matching files from the interpreter source filesystem.
func (interp *Interpreter) resolveEmbed(dir string, patterns []string) (*embedData, error) {
	dirs, files, err := collectEmbedEntries(interp.opt.filesystem, dir)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}

	data := &embedData{dir: dir, patterns: patterns, files: map[string][]byte{}}

	for _, raw := range patterns {
		all, pattern := splitAll(raw)
		if err := validateEmbedPattern(pattern); err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
		matched := false
		add := func(rel string) error {
			if _, ok := data.files[rel]; ok {
				return nil
			}
			full := path.Join(dir, rel)
			b, err := fs.ReadFile(interp.opt.filesystem, full)
			if err != nil {
				return err
			}
			data.files[rel] = b
			matched = true
			return nil
		}

		// Match directories: embed the whole subtree.
		for d := range dirs {
			ok, err := path.Match(pattern, d)
			if err != nil {
				return nil, err
			}
			if !ok {
				continue
			}
			prefix := d + "/"
			for f := range files {
				if f == d || strings.HasPrefix(f, prefix) {
					if all || visible(f) {
						if err := add(f); err != nil {
							return nil, err
						}
					}
				}
			}
		}

		// Match files.
		for f := range files {
			ok, err := path.Match(pattern, f)
			if err != nil {
				return nil, err
			}
			if !ok {
				continue
			}
			if all || visible(f) {
				if err := add(f); err != nil {
					return nil, err
				}
			}
		}

		if !matched {
			return nil, fmt.Errorf("go:embed: pattern %q matched no files", raw)
		}
	}

	return data, nil
}

// genEmbedVar generates the initialization of a global variable declared with
// a //go:embed directive.
func genEmbedVar(n *node) {
	next := getExec(n.tnext)
	data := n.embed
	if data == nil {
		n.gen = nop
		n.gen(n)
		return
	}

	// The embedded declaration must declare a single variable.
	if len(n.child) < 2 || n.nleft != 1 {
		n.exec = func(f *frame) bltn {
			panic(n.cfgErrorf("go:embed cannot apply to var with multiple names"))
		}
		return
	}
	dest := n.child[0]
	index := dest.findex
	level := dest.level
	rt := n.typ.frameType()

	n.exec = func(f *frame) bltn {
		v, err := embedValue(data, rt)
		if err != nil {
			panic(n.cfgErrorf("go:embed: %v", err))
		}
		getFrame(f, level).data[index].Set(v)
		return next
	}
}

// embedValue builds the reflect.Value of the embedded content for the given
// target type.
func embedValue(data *embedData, rt reflect.Type) (reflect.Value, error) {
	switch rt.Kind() {
	case reflect.String:
		if len(data.files) != 1 {
			return reflect.Value{}, fmt.Errorf("pattern matches %d files, but must match exactly one file for a string", len(data.files))
		}
		for _, b := range data.files {
			return reflect.ValueOf(string(b)).Convert(rt), nil
		}
	case reflect.Slice:
		if rt.Elem().Kind() != reflect.Uint8 {
			return reflect.Value{}, fmt.Errorf("go:embed cannot apply to var of type %s", rt)
		}
		if len(data.files) != 1 {
			return reflect.Value{}, fmt.Errorf("pattern matches %d files, but must match exactly one file for a []byte", len(data.files))
		}
		for _, b := range data.files {
			return reflect.ValueOf(b).Convert(rt), nil
		}
	}

	if rt == reflect.TypeOf(EmbedFS{}) {
		fs := EmbedFS{files: data.files}
		return reflect.ValueOf(fs), nil
	}

	return reflect.Value{}, fmt.Errorf("go:embed cannot apply to var of type %s", rt)
}

// EmbedFS is a read-only in-memory filesystem built from //go:embed
// directives. It implements fs.FS, fs.ReadFileFS and fs.ReadDirFS.
type EmbedFS struct {
	files map[string][]byte
}

var (
	_ fs.FS         = (*EmbedFS)(nil)
	_ fs.ReadFileFS = (*EmbedFS)(nil)
	_ fs.ReadDirFS  = (*EmbedFS)(nil)
)

// Open opens the named file or directory for reading.
func (f *EmbedFS) Open(name string) (fs.File, error) {
	if name == "" {
		name = "."
	}
	if !fs.ValidPath(name) && name != "." {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "open", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrInvalid}
	}
	name = path.Clean(name)
	isDir, ok := f.lookup(name)
	if !ok {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "open", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrNotExist}
	}
	if isDir {
		return &embedDir{name: name, entries: f.readDir(name)}, nil
	}
	return &embedFile{name: name, data: append([]byte(nil), f.files[name]...)}, nil
}

// ReadFile reads the named file and returns its contents. A fresh copy is
// returned on every call.
func (f *EmbedFS) ReadFile(name string) ([]byte, error) {
	if !fs.ValidPath(name) {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrInvalid}
	}
	name = path.Clean(name)
	isDir, ok := f.lookup(name)
	if !ok {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrNotExist}
	}
	if isDir {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: errors.New("is a directory")}
	}
	return append([]byte(nil), f.files[name]...), nil
}

// ReadDir reads the named directory and returns a list of directory entries
// sorted by filename.
func (f *EmbedFS) ReadDir(name string) ([]fs.DirEntry, error) {
	if name == "" {
		name = "."
	}
	if !fs.ValidPath(name) && name != "." {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrInvalid}
	}
	name = path.Clean(name)
	isDir, ok := f.lookup(name)
	if !ok {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrNotExist}
	}
	if !isDir {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: errors.New("not a directory")}
	}
	return f.readDir(name), nil
}

func (f *EmbedFS) lookup(name string) (isDir, ok bool) {
	if f.files == nil {
		return false, false
	}
	name = strings.TrimSuffix(name, "/")
	if name == "." {
		return true, len(f.files) > 0
	}
	if _, ok := f.files[name]; ok {
		return false, true
	}
	prefix := name + "/"
	for p := range f.files {
		if strings.HasPrefix(p, prefix) {
			return true, true
		}
	}
	return false, false
}

func (f *EmbedFS) readDir(name string) []fs.DirEntry {
	prefix := ""
	if name != "." {
		prefix = name + "/"
	}
	type entry struct {
		base  string
		isDir bool
	}
	m := map[string]entry{}
	for p := range f.files {
		if !strings.HasPrefix(p, prefix) {
			continue
		}
		rest := p[len(prefix):]
		if rest == "" {
			continue
		}
		base, sub, _ := strings.Cut(rest, "/")
		e, ok := m[base]
		if !ok {
			e = entry{base: base}
		}
		if sub != "" {
			e.isDir = true
		}
		m[base] = e
	}
	names := make([]string, 0, len(m))
	for base := range m {
		names = append(names, base)
	}
	sort.Strings(names)
	entries := make([]fs.DirEntry, len(names))
	for i, base := range names {
		e := m[base]
		entries[i] = &embedDirEntry{name: e.base, isDir: e.isDir, size: 0}
	}
	return entries
}

type embedFile struct {
	name   string
	data   []byte
	offset int64
}

func (f *embedFile) Close() error { return nil }

func (f *embedFile) Read(p []byte) (int, error) {
	if f.offset >= int64(len(f.data)) {
		return 0, io.EOF
	}
	n := copy(p, f.data[f.offset:])
	f.offset += int64(n)
	return n, nil
}

func (f *embedFile) Stat() (fs.FileInfo, error) {
	return &embedFileInfo{name: f.name, size: int64(len(f.data))}, nil
}

func (f *embedFile) Seek(offset int64, whence int) (int64, error) {
	switch whence {
	case io.SeekStart:
		// offset unchanged
	case io.SeekCurrent:
		offset += f.offset
	case io.SeekEnd:
		offset += int64(len(f.data))
	default:
		return 0, &fs.PathError{Op: "seek", Path: f.name, Err: fs.ErrInvalid}
	}
	if offset < 0 || offset > int64(len(f.data)) {
		return 0, &fs.PathError{Op: "seek", Path: f.name, Err: fs.ErrInvalid}
	}
	f.offset = offset
	return offset, nil
}

type embedDir struct {
	name    string
	entries []fs.DirEntry
	offset  int
}

func (d *embedDir) Close() error { return nil }

func (d *embedDir) Read([]byte) (int, error) {
	return 0, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: d.name, Err: errors.New("is a directory")}
}

func (d *embedDir) Stat() (fs.FileInfo, error) {
	return &embedFileInfo{name: d.name, isDir: true}, nil
}

func (d *embedDir) ReadDir(n int) ([]fs.DirEntry, error) {
	if n <= 0 {
		d.offset = 0
		res := make([]fs.DirEntry, len(d.entries))
		copy(res, d.entries)
		return res, nil
	}
	if d.offset >= len(d.entries) {
		return nil, io.EOF
	}
	res := d.entries[d.offset:]
	if n < len(res) {
		res = res[:n]
	}
	d.offset += len(res)
	return res, nil
}

type embedDirEntry struct {
	name  string
	isDir bool
	size  int64
}

func (e *embedDirEntry) Name() string               { return e.name }
func (e *embedDirEntry) IsDir() bool                { return e.isDir }
func (e *embedDirEntry) Type() fs.FileMode          { if e.isDir { return fs.ModeDir }; return 0 }
func (e *embedDirEntry) Info() (fs.FileInfo, error) { return &embedFileInfo{name: e.name, isDir: e.isDir, size: e.size}, nil }

type embedFileInfo struct {
	name  string
	isDir bool
	size  int64
}

func (i *embedFileInfo) Name() string       { return i.name }
func (i *embedFileInfo) Size() int64        { return i.size }
func (i *embedFileInfo) Mode() fs.FileMode  { if i.isDir { return fs.ModeDir }; return 0 }
func (i *embedFileInfo) ModTime() time.Time { return time.Time{} }
func (i *embedFileInfo) IsDir() bool        { return i.isDir }
func (i *embedFileInfo) Sys() interface{}   { return nil }
EOF

func genGlobalVarDecl(nodes []*node, sc *scope) (*node, error) {
	varNode := &node{kind: varDecl, action: aNop, gen: nop}

	deps := map[*node][]*node{}
	for _, n := range nodes {
		deps[n] = getVarDependencies(n, sc)
	}

	inited := map[*node]bool{}
	revisit := []*node{}
	for {
		for _, n := range nodes {
			canInit := true
			for _, d := range deps[n] {
				if !inited[d] {
					canInit = false
				}
			}
			if !canInit {
				revisit = append(revisit, n)
				continue
			}

			varNode.child = append(varNode.child, n)
			inited[n] = true
		}

		if len(revisit) == 0 || equalNodes(nodes, revisit) {
			break
		}

		nodes = revisit
		revisit = []*node{}
	}

	if len(revisit) > 0 {
		return nil, revisit[0].cfgErrorf("variable definition loop")
	}
	wireChild(varNode)
	return varNode, nil
}

func getVarDependencies(nod *node, sc *scope) (deps []*node) {
	nod.Walk(func(n *node) bool {
		if n.kind != identExpr {
			return true
		}
		// Process ident nodes, and avoid false dependencies.
		if n.anc.kind == selectorExpr && childPos(n) == 1 {
			return false
		}
		sym, _, ok := sc.lookup(n.ident)
		if !ok {

// opt stores interpreter options.
type opt struct {
	// dotCmd is the command to process the dot graph produced when astDot and/or
	// cfgDot is enabled. It defaults to 'dot -Tdot -o <filename>.dot'.
	dotCmd       string
	context      build.Context     // build context: GOPATH, build constraints
	stdin        io.Reader         // standard input
	stdout       io.Writer         // standard output
	stderr       io.Writer         // standard error
	args         []string          // cmdline args
	env          map[string]string // environment of interpreter, entries in form of "key=value"
	filesystem   fs.FS             // filesystem containing sources
	astDot       bool              // display AST graph (debug)
	cfgDot       bool              // display CFG graph (debug)
	noRun        bool              // compile, but do not run
	fastChan     bool              // disable cancellable chan operations
	specialStdio bool              // allows os.Stdin, os.Stdout, os.Stderr to not be file descriptors
	unrestricted bool              // allow use of non-sandboxed symbols
}

func (f *frame) clone() *frame {
	f.mutex.RLock()
	defer f.mutex.RUnlock()
	nf := &frame{
		anc:       f.anc,
		root:      f.root,
		deferred:  f.deferred,
		recovered: f.recovered,
		id:        f.runid(),
		done:      f.done,
		debug:     f.debug,
	}
	nf.data = make([]reflect.Value, len(f.data))
	copy(nf.data, f.data)
	return nf
}

STEP 76 SOURCE agent
REASONING_CONTENT
There's `compile_test.go` in package `interp` (internal test package) that imports stdlib. If stdlib imports interp, that's a cycle: interp (with
test) imports stdlib, stdlib imports interp. Go doesn't allow.

So we cannot have stdlib import interp. We need a different approach for exposing the embed FS type to stdlib without importing interp.

Options:
1. Define the EmbedFS type in a separate low-level package that both interp and stdlib can import, without cycle. E.g., create `internal/embedfs`
package. interp imports it; stdlib imports it. But the type needs to be the same as what the interpreter's `embed.FS` resolves to. Since stdlib
registers `reflect.ValueOf((*embedfs.FS)(nil))`, the script's `embed.FS` type becomes `embedfs.FS`. And genEmbedVar compares against `embedfs.FS`.
That works. Let's create `internal/embedfs` package (or just a package in the repo root).

But wait — can stdlib import an internal package? Yes, `internal/` under the module root can be imported by any package within the same module.
`github.com/traefik/yaegi/internal/embedfs` is importable by both interp and stdlib (both within the module). Good.

2. Put EmbedFS type in a new public package like `github.com/traefik/yaegi/embedfs`. That also works and avoids internal import restrictions. But
internal is fine and hides it.

Let me go with `internal/embedfs`.

Actually, wait: There might be an even simpler approach. The interpreter could define its own embed FS type as an interpreted type rather than binary.
But the stdlib symbol registration is the cleanest.

Let me refactor:
- Move `EmbedFS` (and helpers embedFile etc.) to `internal/embedfs` package, exported type `FS`.
- interp/embed.go uses `embedfs.FS` and `embedfs.New` maybe.
- stdlib/embed.go registers `embedfs.FS`.

But the interp package's genEmbedVar needs to construct an `embedfs.FS`. So `embedfs` package needs a constructor: `func New(files map[string][]byte)
FS`. Also `FS` methods.

Let me create the package.

Actually, we could also keep the type in interp but avoid stdlib importing interp by having stdlib's embed.go define a type alias? No, alias would
need import.

Let me refactor. First create internal/embedfs/embedfs.go with the FS implementation (copy from interp/embed.go). Then modify interp/embed.go to
import it, remove the EmbedFS type and its methods from interp, and update genEmbedVar to use embedfs.FS.

// Package embedfs provides a read-only in-memory filesystem used to back
// //go:embed directives in the interpreter.
package embedfs

import (
	"errors"
	"io"
	"io/fs"
	"path"
	"sort"
	"strings"
	"time"
)

// FS is a read-only in-memory filesystem built from //go:embed directives.
// It implements fs.FS, fs.ReadFileFS and fs.ReadDirFS.
type FS struct {
	files map[string][]byte
}

var (
	_ fs.FS         = (*FS)(nil)
	_ fs.ReadFileFS = (*FS)(nil)
	_ fs.ReadDirFS  = (*FS)(nil)
)

// New returns a read-only filesystem containing the given files.
// The keys of files are slash-separated paths relative to the filesystem root.
func New(files map[string][]byte) FS {
	return FS{files: files}
}

// Open opens the named file or directory for reading.
func (f *FS) Open(name string) (fs.File, error) {
	if name == "" {
		name = "."
	}
	if !fs.ValidPath(name) && name != "." {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "open", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrInvalid}
	}
	name = path.Clean(name)
	isDir, ok := f.lookup(name)
	if !ok {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "open", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrNotExist}
	}
	if isDir {
		return &embedDir{name: name, entries: f.readDir(name)}, nil
	}
	return &embedFile{name: name, data: append([]byte(nil), f.files[name]...)}, nil
}

// ReadFile reads the named file and returns its contents. A fresh copy is
// returned on every call.
func (f *FS) ReadFile(name string) ([]byte, error) {
	if !fs.ValidPath(name) {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrInvalid}
	}
	name = path.Clean(name)
	isDir, ok := f.lookup(name)
	if !ok {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrNotExist}
	}
	if isDir {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: errors.New("is a directory")}
	}
	return append([]byte(nil), f.files[name]...), nil
}

// ReadDir reads the named directory and returns a list of directory entries
// sorted by filename.
func (f *FS) ReadDir(name string) ([]fs.DirEntry, error) {
	if name == "" {
		name = "."
	}
	if !fs.ValidPath(name) && name != "." {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrInvalid}
	}
	name = path.Clean(name)
	isDir, ok := f.lookup(name)
	if !ok {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrNotExist}
	}
	if !isDir {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: errors.New("not a directory")}
	}
	return f.readDir(name), nil
}

func (f *FS) lookup(name string) (isDir, ok bool) {
	if f.files == nil {
		return false, false
	}
	name = strings.TrimSuffix(name, "/")
	if name == "." {
		return true, len(f.files) > 0
	}
	if _, ok := f.files[name]; ok {
		return false, true
	}
	prefix := name + "/"
	for p := range f.files {
		if strings.HasPrefix(p, prefix) {
			return true, true
		}
	}
	return false, false
}

func (f *FS) readDir(name string) []fs.DirEntry {
	prefix := ""
	if name != "." {
		prefix = name + "/"
	}
	type entry struct {
		base  string
		isDir bool
	}
	m := map[string]entry{}
	for p := range f.files {
		if !strings.HasPrefix(p, prefix) {
			continue
		}
		rest := p[len(prefix):]
		if rest == "" {
			continue
		}
		base, sub, _ := strings.Cut(rest, "/")
		e, ok := m[base]
		if !ok {
			e = entry{base: base}
		}
		if sub != "" {
			e.isDir = true
		}
		m[base] = e
	}
	names := make([]string, 0, len(m))
	for base := range m {
		names = append(names, base)
	}
	sort.Strings(names)
	entries := make([]fs.DirEntry, len(names))
	for i, base := range names {
		e := m[base]
		entries[i] = &embedDirEntry{name: e.base, isDir: e.isDir, size: 0}
	}
	return entries
}

type embedFile struct {
	name   string
	data   []byte
	offset int64
}

func (f *embedFile) Close() error { return nil }

func (f *embedFile) Read(p []byte) (int, error) {
	if f.offset >= int64(len(f.data)) {
		return 0, io.EOF
	}
	n := copy(p, f.data[f.offset:])
	f.offset += int64(n)
	return n, nil
}

func (f *embedFile) Stat() (fs.FileInfo, error) {
	return &embedFileInfo{name: f.name, size: int64(len(f.data))}, nil
}

func (f *embedFile) Seek(offset int64, whence int) (int64, error) {
	switch whence {
	case io.SeekStart:
		// offset unchanged
	case io.SeekCurrent:
		offset += f.offset
	case io.SeekEnd:
		offset += int64(len(f.data))
	default:
		return 0, &fs.PathError{Op: "seek", Path: f.name, Err: fs.ErrInvalid}
	}
	if offset < 0 || offset > int64(len(f.data)) {
		return 0, &fs.PathError{Op: "seek", Path: f.name, Err: fs.ErrInvalid}
	}
	f.offset = offset
	return offset, nil
}

type embedDir struct {
	name    string
	entries []fs.DirEntry
	offset  int
}

func (d *embedDir) Close() error { return nil }

func (d *embedDir) Read([]byte) (int, error) {
	return 0, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: d.name, Err: errors.New("is a directory")}
}

func (d *embedDir) Stat() (fs.FileInfo, error) {
	return &embedFileInfo{name: d.name, isDir: true}, nil
}

func (d *embedDir) ReadDir(n int) ([]fs.DirEntry, error) {
	if n <= 0 {
		d.offset = 0
		res := make([]fs.DirEntry, len(d.entries))
		copy(res, d.entries)
		return res, nil
	}
	if d.offset >= len(d.entries) {
		return nil, io.EOF
	}
	res := d.entries[d.offset:]
	if n < len(res) {
		res = res[:n]
	}
	d.offset += len(res)
	return res, nil
}

type embedDirEntry struct {
	name  string
	isDir bool
	size  int64
}

func (e *embedDirEntry) Name() string               { return e.name }
func (e *embedDirEntry) IsDir() bool                { return e.isDir }
func (e *embedDirEntry) Type() fs.FileMode          { if e.isDir { return fs.ModeDir }; return 0 }
func (e *embedDirEntry) Info() (fs.FileInfo, error) { return &embedFileInfo{name: e.name, isDir: e.isDir, size: e.size}, nil }

type embedFileInfo struct {
	name  string
	isDir bool
	size  int64
}

func (i *embedFileInfo) Name() string       { return i.name }
func (i *embedFileInfo) Size() int64        { return i.size }
func (i *embedFileInfo) Mode() fs.FileMode  { if i.isDir { return fs.ModeDir }; return 0 }
func (i *embedFileInfo) ModTime() time.Time { return time.Time{} }
func (i *embedFileInfo) IsDir() bool        { return i.isDir }
func (i *embedFileInfo) Sys() interface{}   { return nil }
EOF

package interp

import (

// embedData stores the resolved files of a //go:embed directive.
type embedData struct {
	dir      string
	patterns []string
	files    map[string][]byte
}

// embedPatterns extracts the //go:embed directives from a comment group.
// It returns the list of patterns found in all the embed lines.
func embedPatterns(cg *ast.CommentGroup) ([]string, error) {
	if cg == nil {
		return nil, nil
	}
	var patterns []string
	for _, c := range cg.List {
		text := c.Text
		if len(text) < len("//go:embed") || text[:len("//go:embed")] != "//go:embed" {
			continue
		}
		rest := text[len("//go:embed"):]
		if rest != "" && rest[0] != ' ' && rest[0] != '\t' {
			// Not a go:embed directive, just a comment starting with go:embed.
			continue
		}
		ps, err := parseEmbedPatterns(rest)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
		patterns = append(patterns, ps...)
	}
	return patterns, nil

package interp_test

import (
	"bytes"
	"io/fs"
	"testing"
	"testing/fstest"

	"github.com/traefik/yaegi/interp"
	"github.com/traefik/yaegi/stdlib"
)

func runEmbed(t *testing.T, fsys fs.FS, src string) (string, error) {
	t.Helper()
	i := interp.New(interp.Options{SourcecodeFilesystem: fsys})
	if err := i.Use(stdlib.Symbols); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	var stdout bytes.Buffer
	i.Stdout = &stdout
	_, err := i.EvalPath("main.go")
	return stdout.String(), err
}

func TestEmbedString(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	_ "embed"
	"fmt"
)
//go:embed hello.txt
var s string
func main() { fmt.Println(s) }
`)},
		"hello.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("hello world")},
	}
	out, err := runEmbed(t, fsys, "")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if out != "hello world\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", out)
	}
}

func TestEmbedBytes(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	_ "embed"
	"fmt"
)
//go:embed hello.txt
var b []byte
func main() { fmt.Println(string(b)) }
`)},
		"hello.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("hello bytes")},
	}
	out, err := runEmbed(t, fsys, "")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if out != "hello bytes\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", out)
	}
}

func TestEmbedFS(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	"embed"
	"fmt"
	"io/fs"
)
//go:embed data
var content embed.FS
func main() {
	b, err := content.ReadFile("data/a.txt")
	if err != nil { fmt.Println("ERR", err); return }
	fmt.Println(string(b))
	entries, _ := fs.ReadDir(content, "data")
	fmt.Println(len(entries), entries[0].Name())
	var _ fs.FS = content
	var _ fs.ReadFileFS = content
	var _ fs.ReadDirFS = content
}
`)},
		"data/a.txt":   &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("A")},
		"data/b.txt":   &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("B")},
		"data/sub/c.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("C")},
	}
	out, err := runEmbed(t, fsys, "")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if out != "A\n2 a.txt\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", out)
	}
}

func TestEmbedGrouped(t *testing.T) {

		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	_ "embed"
	"fmt"
)
//go:embed a.txt
var (
	x string
	//go:embed b.txt
	y string
)
func main() { fmt.Println(x, y) }
`)},
		"a.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("A")},
		"b.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("B")},
	}
	out, err := runEmbed(t, fsys, "")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if out != "A B\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", out)
	}
}

func TestEmbedDirAndHidden(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	"embed"
	"fmt"
)
//go:embed all:data
var content embed.FS
func main() {
	entries, _ := content.ReadDir("data")
	fmt.Println(len(entries), entries[0].Name())
	b, err := content.ReadFile("data/.hidden")
	if err == nil { fmt.Println("hidden", string(b)) } else { fmt.Println("hidden ERR") }
}
`)},
		"data/a.txt":      &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("A")},
		"data/.hidden":    &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("H")},
	}
	out, err := runEmbed(t, fsys, "")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if out != "2 .hidden\nhidden H\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", out)
	}
}

func TestEmbedErrors(t *testing.T) {
	tests := []struct {
		name    string
		files   map[string]string
		wantErr string
	}{
		{
			name: "no files",
			files: map[string]string{
				"main.go": `package main
import _ "embed"
//go:embed missing.txt
var s string
func main() {}
`,
			},
			wantErr: "no files",
		},
		{
			name: "multiple files for string",
			files: map[string]string{
				"main.go": `package main
import _ "embed"
//go:embed data
var s string
func main() {}
`,
				"data/a.txt": "A",
				"data/b.txt": "B",
			},
			wantErr: "exactly one file",
		},
		{
			name: "initializer",
			files: map[string]string{
				"main.go": `package main
import _ "embed"
//go:embed a.txt
var s = "x"
func main() {}
`,
				"a.txt": "A",
			},
			wantErr: "initializer",
		},
	}
	for _, tc := range tests {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			fsys := fstest.MapFS{}
			for k, v := range tc.files {
				fsys[k] = &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(v)}
			}
			_, err := runEmbed(t, fsys, "")
			if err == nil || !bytes.Contains([]byte(err.Error()), []byte(tc.wantErr)) {
				t.Fatalf("got err %v, want containing %q", err, tc.wantErr)
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestEmbedNotOverwritten(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	_ "embed"
	"fmt"
)
//go:embed a.txt
var s string
var copy = s
func main() { fmt.Println(s, copy) }
`)},
		"a.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("AA")},
	}
	out, err := runEmbed(t, fsys, "")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if out != "AA AA\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", out)
	}
}
EOF

the GenDecl, then its children (specs). If we return false on a ValueSpec, Inspect skips that node's children but continues to the next child of
GenDecl. Eventually returns false? No, returning false from the callback stops traversal of that node's subtree only, not the entire Inspect. So the

leaving a node. If we return false from a non-nil node, does Inspect still call the callback with nil for that node? In `ast.Inspect`, the
implementation: it calls visitor(node); if !visitor(node) return; then visits children; then visitor(nil). If visitor returns false, it does NOT call
visitor(nil) for that node, so the stack is NOT popped! That leaves the stack inconsistent, causing later `anc` to be wrong, and could cause panic.

Let me check: In the ValueSpec case, after we set err and return false, maybe the code path that handles "no files" is in `resolveEmbed`, but we
assign `data, err := interp.resolveEmbed(...)`. Here `err` is a NEW variable via `:=`? Actually inside the if block, we have:

data, err := interp.resolveEmbed(dir, patterns)
```
But `err` was already declared at the top of the callback? The callback's `err` is the outer `err` variable (from ast()). `data, err := ...` inside
the if block creates NEW `data` and reuses `err` (since err already exists in the outer scope but within the if block, `:=` creates new variables for
data and reuses existing err? Actually in Go, `:=` in an inner block creates new variables for all left-hand names that are not already declared in
the SAME block. `err` is declared in the function scope (outer block), not the if block. So `data, err :=` inside the if block creates a NEW `err`
local to the if block, shadowing the outer err! Then after the if block, the outer err remains nil. That's the bug! Same for `patterns, err :=
embedPatterns(a.Doc)` — that also shadows err.

So the errors are set on a shadowed err, and the outer err stays nil → ast() returns nil error → proceeds.

		case *ast.ValueSpec:
			kind := valueSpec
			act := aNop
			switch {
			case a.Values != nil:
				if len(a.Names) > 1 && len(a.Values) == 1 {
					if anc.node.kind == constDecl || anc.node.kind == varDecl {
						kind = defineXStmt
					} else {
						kind = assignXStmt
					}
					act = aAssignX
				} else {
					if anc.node.kind == constDecl || anc.node.kind == varDecl {
						kind = defineStmt
					} else {
						kind = assignStmt
					}
					act = aAssign
				}
			case anc.node.kind == constDecl:
				kind, act = defineStmt, aAssign

				kind, act = defineStmt, aAssign
			}
			n := addChild(&root, anc, pos, kind, act)

					data, err := interp.resolveEmbed(dir, patterns)
					if err != nil {
						err = astError(err)
						return false
					}
					n.embed = data
				}
			}

(error) is correct. However, the feature request says "in both standalone and grouped var ( ... ) forms". For grouped form, the embed directive should

directive is before the first spec inside the group:

        x string
        //go:embed b.txt
        y string

```

message can be "misplaced go:embed directive". Let me update the message for accuracy.

Now TestEmbedFS: "cannot use type embedfs.FS as type fs.FS". This is the interface satisfaction problem. Let me investigate how yaegi handles binary
type implementing interfaces.

In yaegi, when you do `var _ fs.FS = content`, the compiler checks assignability. For a source type `embedfs.FS` (binary type) being assigned to an
interface `fs.FS` (binary interface), yaegi needs to know that embedfs.FS implements fs.FS. This is handled by "interface wrapper" mechanisms in
yaegi. When a binary type's methods are available, the interpreter can check method sets. But `fs.FS` is an interface in binPkg with methods.
`embedfs.FS` has method `Open`. Since we exported the type via `reflect.ValueOf((*embedfs.FS)(nil))`, the interpreter's itype for it should include
methods from reflection. Does it? Let me look at how valueTOf and binary method lookup work.

func (t *itype) assignableTo(o *itype) bool {
	if t.equals(o) {
		return true
	}

	if t.cat == linkedT && o.cat == linkedT && (t.underlying().id() != o.underlying().id() || !typeDefined(t, o)) {
		return false
	}

	if t.isNil() && o.hasNil() || o.isNil() && t.hasNil() {
		return true
	}

	if t.TypeOf().AssignableTo(o.TypeOf()) {
		return true
	}

	if isInterface(o) && t.implements(o) {
		return true
	}

	if t.cat == sliceT && o.cat == sliceT {
		return t.val.assignableTo(o.val)
	}

	if t.isBinMethod && isFunc(o) {
		// TODO (marc): check that t without receiver as first parameter is equivalent to o.
		return true
	}

	if t.untyped && isNumber(t.TypeOf()) && isNumber(o.TypeOf()) {
		// Assignability depends on constant numeric value (overflow check), to be tested elsewhere.
		return true
	}

	n := t.node
	if n == nil || !n.rval.IsValid() {
		return false
	}
	con, ok := n.rval.Interface().(constant.Value)
	if !ok {
		return false
	}
	if con == nil || !isConstType(o) {
		return false
	}
	return representableConst(con, o.TypeOf())
}

// convertibleTo returns true if t is convertible to o.
func (t *itype) convertibleTo(o *itype) bool {
	if t.assignableTo(o) {
		return true
	}

	// unsafe checks
	tt, ot := t.TypeOf(), o.TypeOf()
	if (tt.Kind() == reflect.Ptr || tt.Kind() == reflect.Uintptr) && ot.Kind() == reflect.UnsafePointer {
		return true
	}
	if tt.Kind() == reflect.UnsafePointer && (ot.Kind() == reflect.Ptr || ot.Kind() == reflect.Uintptr) {
		return true
	}

	return t.TypeOf().ConvertibleTo(o.TypeOf())
}

// ordered returns true if the type is ordered.
func (t *itype) ordered() bool {
=== implements ===
func (t *itype) implements(it *itype) bool {
	if isBin(t) {
		// Note: in case of a valueInterfaceType, we
		// miss required data which will be available
		// later, so we optimistically return true to progress,
		// and additional checks will be hopefully performed at
		// runtime.
		if rt := it.TypeOf(); rt == valueInterfaceType {
			return true
		}
		return t.TypeOf().Implements(it.TypeOf())
	}
	return t.methods().contains(it.methods())
}

// defaultType returns the default type of an untyped type.
func (t *itype) defaultType(v reflect.Value, sc *scope) *itype {
	if !t.untyped {
		return t
	}

	typ := t
	// The default type can also be derived from a constant value.
	if v.IsValid() && v.Type().Implements(constVal) {
		switch v.Interface().(constant.Value).Kind() {
		case constant.String:
			typ = sc.getType("string")
		case constant.Bool:
			typ = sc.getType("bool")
		case constant.Int:
			switch t.cat {
			case int32T:
				typ = sc.getType("int32")
			default:
				typ = sc.getType("int")
			}
		case constant.Float:
			typ = sc.getType("float64")
		case constant.Complex:
			typ = sc.getType("complex128")
		}
	}

func valueTOf(rtype reflect.Type, opts ...itypeOption) *itype {
	t := &itype{cat: valueT, rtype: rtype, str: rtype.String()}
	for _, opt := range opts {
		opt(t)
	}
	if t.untyped {
		t.str = "untyped " + t.str
	}
	return t
}

// wrapperValueTOf returns a valueT itype wrapping an itype.
func wrapperValueTOf(rtype reflect.Type, val *itype, opts ...itypeOption) *itype {
	t := &itype{cat: valueT, rtype: rtype, val: val, str: rtype.String()}
	for _, opt := range opts {
		opt(t)
	}
	return t
}

func variadicOf(val *itype, opts ...itypeOption) *itype {
	t := &itype{cat: variadicT, val: val, str: "..." + val.str}
	for _, opt := range opts {
		opt(t)
	}
	return t
}

// ptrOf returns a pointer to t.
func ptrOf(val *itype, opts ...itypeOption) *itype {
	if val.ptr != nil {
		return val.ptr
	}
	t := &itype{cat: ptrT, val: val, str: "*" + val.str}
	for _, opt := range opts {
		opt(t)
	}
	val.ptr = t
	return t
}

REASONING_CONTENT
`embedfs.FS` does NOT implement `fs.FS`! Why? Let me check the method signature. `Open(name string) (fs.File, error)` — fs.FS requires `Open(name
string) (fs.File, error)`. Our method is `func (f *FS) Open(name string) (fs.File, error)` — pointer receiver! So `embedfs.FS` (value type) does NOT
implement fs.FS; only `*embedfs.FS` does.

The real `embed.FS` uses value receiver: `func (f FS) Open(...)`. We defined methods on pointer receiver `*FS`. So the value type doesn't implement
the interfaces.

We need to use value receivers for the FS methods (Open, ReadFile, ReadDir). Since the map is reference type, value receiver is fine.

Let me change all methods from `func (f *FS)` to `func (f FS)`. The internal helpers can stay pointer or value. Let me update
internal/embedfs/embedfs.go.

REASONING_CONTENT
While that runs, let me continue improving. Let me think about edge cases and add compile-time validation for the "exactly one file" for
string/[]byte.

Also, let me consider the `importSrc` path for imported packages: the `parse` call passes `name` = path.Join(dir, name) where dir is e.g. "foo/bar".
`embedDirFor` returns path.Dir(name) = "foo/bar". resolveEmbed reads from filesystem at that dir. Good.

But there's a subtle issue: in `importSrc`, when `parse` is called with `inc=false`, and we now always parse comments, that's fine.

Now, another important edge: the `//go:embed` directive must be recognized even when there are blank lines or other comments between the directive and
the var declaration. In Go, only blank lines and `//` comments are permitted between the directive and declaration. The parser groups consecutive
comments; if there's a blank line, it becomes a separate CommentGroup, and the directive might not be in `GenDecl.Doc`. Actually Go's parser
associates the last comment group immediately preceding the declaration as Doc. If there's a blank line between `//go:embed` and `var`, the comment
group is still attached? Let me check: In Go, a doc comment must be immediately preceding (no blank line) to be attached to a declaration. If there's
a blank line, the comment is not in GenDecl.Doc. But Go's embed directive requires it immediately precedes the declaration (blank lines not allowed
between directive and declaration). So we don't need to handle blank lines.

But what about multiple embed lines with other comments in between? E.g.:
```go
//go:embed a.txt
// some comment
//go:embed b.txt
var s string
```
All are in one CommentGroup (no blank lines), and our embedPatterns extracts all go:embed lines. Good.

Now, let me think about the `all:` prefix and directory matching more carefully. Our `visible` function checks all path components. For `all:data`, we
set all=true, so visibility isn't checked. Good. For `data` (no all), we include visible files under data. Good.

But there's a subtle bug: When matching a directory pattern, we iterate `for f := range files` and check `f == d || strings.HasPrefix(f, prefix)`. But
if `d` is a directory that also has a file entry in `files`? Directories and files are separate maps, so no overlap. Good.

Another subtlety: a pattern may match both a file and a directory. E.g., pattern `data` matches directory `data`, includes tree. Fine. Pattern `*`
matches all top-level files AND top-level dirs. In Go, `*` matches everything in the current dir including subdirectories (and embeds their trees).
Our code: `for d := range dirs { if path.Match("*", d) }` — `path.Match("*", "data")` is true (top-level dir), so we include its tree. And files
top-level included. Good.

But wait: `path.Match("*", "data")` — does `*` match `data`? Yes, `*` matches any sequence of non-Separator characters. So it matches top-level dirs
and files. Good.

Now, what about a pattern `data/*` matching `data/sub` (dir) and including `data/sub/c.txt`? path.Match("data/*", "data/sub") true, so include tree
under data/sub → data/sub/c.txt. Good.

Now, the `collectEmbedEntries` walks the whole tree. For a large tree this could be expensive but fine.

Now, the "no files" error: we currently return error from `resolveEmbed` per-pattern if that pattern matches no files. This happens at ast() time,
which propagates correctly. Good.

But there's a subtle issue: if a pattern matches a directory that exists but has no visible files, `matched` stays false and we return "pattern
matched no files". Good.

Now, let's add compile-time validation for string/[]byte exactly-one-file. In cfg valueSpec case, after n.typ is set, we can check:
```go
if n.embed != nil {
    rt := n.typ.frameType()
    if rt.Kind() == reflect.String || (rt.Kind() == reflect.Slice && rt.Elem().Kind() == reflect.Uint8) {
        if len(n.embed.files) != 1 {
            err = n.cfgErrorf("go:embed: pattern matches %d files, must match exactly one for %s", len(n.embed.files), rt)
            return
        }
    } else if rt != reflect.TypeOf(embedfs.FS{}) {
        err = n.cfgErrorf("go:embed cannot apply to var of type %s", rt)
        return
    }
}
```
This gives compile-time errors and avoids the runtime panic. Let me add this to cfg.go valueSpec case. But wait, the valueSpec case is inside a Walk;
returning from the callback with err set. Let me look at the exact code to insert.

		case valueSpec:
			n.gen = reset
			l := len(n.child) - 1
			if n.typ = n.child[l].typ; n.typ == nil {
				if n.typ, err = nodeType(interp, sc, n.child[l]); err != nil {

				c.findex = index
			}
		}
	})

	if sc != interp.universe {
		sc.pop()
	}
	return initNodes, err

func TestEmbedMultipleLines(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	"embed"
	"fmt"
)
//go:embed a.txt
//go:embed b.txt
var f embed.FS
func main() {
	a, _ := f.ReadFile("a.txt")
	b, _ := f.ReadFile("b.txt")
	fmt.Println(string(a), string(b))
}
`)},
		"a.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("A")},
		"b.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("B")},
	}
	out, err := runEmbed(t, fsys, "")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if out != "A B\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", out)
	}
}

func TestEmbedSubdirSource(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"pkg/main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	_ "embed"
	"fmt"
)
//go:embed data.txt
var s string
func main() { fmt.Println(s) }
`)},
		"pkg/data.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("subdir data")},
	}
	i := interp.New(interp.Options{SourcecodeFilesystem: fsys})
	if err := i.Use(stdlib.Symbols); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	var stdout bytes.Buffer
	i.Stdout = &stdout
	_, err := i.EvalPath("pkg/main.go")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if stdout.String() != "subdir data\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", stdout.String())
	}
}

func TestEmbedReadFileCopy(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	"embed"
	"fmt"
)
//go:embed a.txt
var f embed.FS
func main() {
	b1, _ := f.ReadFile("a.txt")
	b2, _ := f.ReadFile("a.txt")
	b1[0] = 'X'
	b3, _ := f.ReadFile("a.txt")
	fmt.Println(string(b2), string(b3))
}
`)},
		"a.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("abc")},
	}
	out, err := runEmbed(t, fsys, "")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if out != "abc abc\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", out)
	}
}

func TestEmbedReadDirFile(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	"embed"
	"fmt"
	"io/fs"
)
//go:embed data
var f embed.FS
func main() {
	d, err := f.Open("data")
	if err != nil { fmt.Println("ERR", err); return }
	rd, ok := d.(fs.ReadDirFile)
	if !ok { fmt.Println("not ReadDirFile"); return }
	entries, err := rd.ReadDir(-1)
	if err != nil { fmt.Println("ERR2", err); return }
	fmt.Println(len(entries), entries[0].Name())
}
`)},
		"data/a.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("A")},
		"data/b.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("B")},
	}
	out, err := runEmbed(t, fsys, "")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if out != "2 a.txt\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", out)
	}
}

func TestEmbedRootReadDir(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	"embed"
	"fmt"
	"io/fs"
)
//go:embed a.txt
var f embed.FS
func main() {
	entries, err := fs.ReadDir(f, ".")
	if err != nil { fmt.Println("ERR", err); return }
	fmt.Println(len(entries), entries[0].Name())
}
`)},
		"a.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("A")},
	}
	out, err := runEmbed(t, fsys, "")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if out != "1 a.txt\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", out)
	}
}

func TestEmbedPatternTopLevelOnly(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	"embed"
	"fmt"
	"io/fs"
)
//go:embed *.txt
var f embed.FS
func main() {
	entries, _ := fs.ReadDir(f, ".")
	fmt.Println(len(entries))
	_, err := f.ReadFile("sub/x.txt")
	if err == nil { fmt.Println("ERR") } else { fmt.Println("not included") }
}
`)},
		"a.txt":      &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("A")},
		"sub/x.txt":  &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("X")},
	}
	out, err := runEmbed(t, fsys, "")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if out != "1\nnot included\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", out)
	}
}

func TestEmbedStringMultipleCompileError(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import _ "embed"
//go:embed data
var s string
func main() {}
`)},
		"data/a.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("A")},
		"data/b.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("B")},
	}
	_, err := runEmbed(t, fsys, "")
	if err == nil || !bytes.Contains([]byte(err.Error()), []byte("exactly one file")) {
		t.Fatalf("got err %v, want exactly one file", err)
	}
}

Let me review for potential issues:

### Issue: REPL mode (inc=true)
In `parse` with inc=true, source is wrapped in `package main;`. The `firstToken` may return token.VAR for `//go:embed`? Actually `firstToken` scans
the source; a comment before var... Let me check firstToken. For REPL, a var declaration with embed:
```
//go:embed hello.txt
var s string
```
firstToken might return token.VAR (comments skipped). Then src = "package main;" + original. Parser attaches the comment to the var declaration. Our
GenDecl handling works. But in REPL, `interp.name` is "_.go" and dir ".". Good.

However, there's a subtle issue in REPL: if the user types an embed directive alone (just `//go:embed hello.txt`), firstToken would be... comment,
then no declaration; probably falls to default and wraps in func main, which would make it a local context? Actually the directive alone isn't valid.
Not our concern.

### Issue: `parse` now always parses comments
For `inc=true`, previously ParseComments was only set in inc mode. Now always. This means `f.Comments` is populated and `setYaegiTags` called for
non-inc too. That's likely fine — actually it enables yaegi:tags comments in EvalPath, which is arguably a feature. But could it break existing tests?
Possibly tests that have comments and expect no tags? Unlikely.

### Issue: `buildOk` parses comments separately and may call setYaegiTags. Then parse() also calls setYaegiTags. Duplicate set is idempotent (append
unique). Fine.

### Issue: In `genGlobalVarDecl`, we set `n.gen = genEmbedVar` for nodes with embed. But `genGlobalVarDecl` may be called multiple times (e.g.,
Execute called twice on same Program). Each time it resets gen to genEmbedVar. Fine.

### Issue: `getVars` collects `child.child...` for varDecl. For a grouped var with multiple ValueSpecs, each ValueSpec is a child of the varDecl, and
getVars returns all. Our embed data on each ValueSpec works. Good.

### Issue: For `var x string` with no embed and no initializer in a grouped var, the valueSpec is generated by `reset`, which allocates a new zero
value. Good.

### Issue: Type checking for `embed.FS` with alias. Go allows `type MyFS = embed.FS`. Our type check uses `rt != reflect.TypeOf(embedfs.FS{})`. An
alias would have the same TypeOf, so works.

### Issue: Named string types. `type MyString string; //go:embed a.txt; var s MyString`. rt.Kind() == reflect.String, and embedValue does
`reflect.ValueOf(string(b)).Convert(rt)`. Works.

### Issue: Named byte slice types. `type MyBytes []byte`. rt.Kind() == Slice, Elem Uint8. Works.

### Issue: `var s [N]byte` with embed — real Go doesn't allow; our type check rejects (not string, not []byte, not FS). Good.

### Issue: `import "embed"` vs `import _ "embed"`. Both work because binPkg has the package.

### Issue: The `stdlib/embed.go` file — will it conflict with generated stdlib files for embed if someone regenerates? The generator list doesn't
include embed, so no conflict. Good.

### Issue: `go vet`/lint might complain about the `embed` field name shadowing package embed? It's a field, fine.

### Issue: `genEmbedVar` uses `getFrame(f, level).data[index].Set(v)`. For a global valueSpec, `level` is `globalFrame` (set in cfg). Let me check
`getFrame` handles globalFrame. Let me look.

func getFrame(f *frame, l int) *frame {

func TestEmbedInitFunction(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	_ "embed"
	"fmt"
)
//go:embed a.txt
var s string
func init() { fmt.Println("init", s) }
func main() { fmt.Println("main", s) }
`)},
		"a.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("AA")},
	}
	out, err := runEmbed(t, fsys, "")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if out != "init AA\nmain AA\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", out)
	}
}

func TestEmbedImportedPackage(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	"fmt"
	"./mypkg"
)
func main() { fmt.Println(mypkg.Get()) }
`)},
		"mypkg/mypkg.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package mypkg
import _ "embed"
//go:embed data.txt
var S string
func Get() string { return S }
`)},
		"mypkg/data.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("from pkg")},
	}
	i := interp.New(interp.Options{SourcecodeFilesystem: fsys, GoPath: "."})
	if err := i.Use(stdlib.Symbols); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	var stdout bytes.Buffer
	i.Stdout = &stdout
	_, err := i.EvalPath("main.go")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if stdout.String() != "from pkg\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", stdout.String())
	}
}

Still running (the suite is large; it has 979 _test files + unit tests). It may take several minutes. Let me continue refining.

Let me review `interp/embed.go` for any remaining issues:

1. `embedPatterns` checks `text[:len("//go:embed")] != "//go:embed"`. If a comment is `/*go:embed ... */` (block), it won't match. That's fine per Go
(only line comments).

2. `parseEmbedPatterns` handles quoted patterns. But if a quoted pattern is followed by another token without whitespace, e.g. `"a.txt"b.txt`, our
code would parse `"a.txt"` then see `b.txt` and parse it as separate. Go would error. Not a big deal.

3. `resolveEmbed` validates each pattern and returns error if no match. But it uses `for d := range dirs` which iterates map in random order;
deterministic not needed for matching.

4. `collectEmbedEntries` uses `fs.ReadDir` on the source filesystem. For realFS, `fs.ReadDir(fsys, dir)` where dir is e.g. "." or "pkg".
realFS.Open(".", "pkg") uses os.Open which works. But for MapFS, fs.ReadDir works on implicit dirs. Good.

5. Potential issue: `fs.ReadDir` on a file path (if dir is a file) returns error; fine.

6. `visible` function: checks all path components. For a top-level file "a.txt", visible. For "data/.hidden", false. For "data/sub", visible. Good.

Now, let me think about the `all:` handling more. Go's `all:` prefix applies to the pattern, so `all:data/*` includes hidden files under data. Our
`all` flag applies to all matches from that pattern. Good.

But there's a subtlety: In Go, `all:` only affects the walking of directories, not the initial pattern match? Actually `all:` is documented as "If a
pattern begins with the prefix all:, then the rule for walking directories is changed to include those files beginning with . or _." So our
implementation (all affects visibility of all included files) is correct.

Now, one thing I should double check: the `embed.FS` type's methods are on value receiver now, so `reflect.TypeOf(embedfs.FS{})` implements fs.FS.
Good. But in `embedValue`, we create `embedfs.New(data.files)` which returns a value `FS`. `reflect.ValueOf(embedfs.New(data.files))` is of type
`embedfs.FS`. Good.

Now, let me think about how the interpreted code accesses `embed.FS` methods:
- `content.ReadFile("...")`: This is a method call on a binary type value. In yaegi, when you call a method on a binary type value, it uses
reflection. Since `embedfs.FS` has methods Open, ReadFile, ReadDir, the calls should work. TestEmbedFS verified direct `content.ReadFile` works, and
`fs.ReadDir(content, ...)` works (which uses the ReadDirFS interface method via reflection? Actually fs.ReadDir is a binary function that type-asserts
to fs.ReadDirFS and calls ReadDir — works because our type implements the interface). Good.

- What about `content.Open(...)` and then `.ReadDir`? TestEmbedReadDirFile uses `f.Open("data")` and type-asserts to fs.ReadDirFile. It worked, so
Open returns a value that implements fs.ReadDirFile. Good.

Now, let me check: does `content.Open("data")` return `*embedDir` (pointer) which implements fs.ReadDirFile via pointer receiver? We defined `ReadDir`
on `*embedDir`, and `Open` returns `&embedDir{...}` (pointer). `fs.ReadDirFile` requires `ReadDir(n int) ([]fs.DirEntry, error)`. Our pointer receiver
satisfies it. Good.

Now, let me consider the `embed.FS` value in interpreted code with `var _ fs.FS = content` — passed. Good.

### Potential issue: The `embed` package import in stdlib may conflict with actual stdlib generation when `go generate` is run

Since we added `stdlib/embed.go` in package stdlib, and the extract tool generates files into the same package with names like `go1_21_embed.go` if
embed were in the list. It's not in the list, so no conflict. But if someone adds embed to the generate list, there'd be duplicate
`Symbols["embed/embed"]` assignments. That's a future concern, not now.

### Potential issue: `go mod` / build tags

`stdlib/embed.go` has `//go:build go1.21`. go.mod says go 1.21, so it's always included for the supported Go versions (1.21+). Good.

Now, let me think about whether the `//go:embed` directive should require importing "embed". In real Go, yes. Our implementation doesn't enforce it.
If a script uses `//go:embed` without importing embed, real Go errors "missing import of embed". Should we enforce? The feature request doesn't
explicitly say to enforce import. It says target types include string/[]byte/embed.FS. Since we add the embed package, scripts that follow Go will
import it. But a script using `//go:embed` with string and no `import _ "embed"` would work in our implementation but not in real Go. Should we add
the check? It might be nice for Go compatibility but requires tracking imports. Since the requirement doesn't mention it, I'll leave it — being more
permissive is acceptable.

Actually, wait: if a script has `//go:embed` but no import of embed, our `Use(stdlib.Symbols)` still works because we don't require import. Fine.

`firstToken` is used in REPL to decide wrapping. With comments now always parsed, `firstToken` scans and returns first token. For a line starting with
`//go:embed`, firstToken would skip comments and return VAR. Good.

func (interp *Interpreter) firstToken(src string) token.Token {
	var s scanner.Scanner
	file := interp.fset.AddFile("", interp.fset.Base(), len(src))
	s.Init(file, []byte(src), nil, 0)

	_, tok, _ := s.Scan()
	return tok
}

func ignoreError(err error, src string) bool {
	se, ok := err.(scanner.ErrorList)
	if !ok {
		return false
	}
	if len(se) == 0 {
		return false
	}
	return ignoreScannerError(se[0], src)
}

func wrapInMain(src string) string {
	return fmt.Sprintf("package main; func main() {%s\n}", src)
}

Still running. It's been ~2 minutes. The full interp test suite is huge. Let me wait while doing more code review.

Let me review the `genEmbedVar` function more carefully:

```go
func genEmbedVar(n *node) {
        next := getExec(n.tnext)
        data := n.embed
        if data == nil {
                n.gen = nop
                n.gen(n)
                return
        }

        if len(n.child) < 2 || n.nleft != 1 {
                n.exec = func(f *frame) bltn {
                        panic(n.cfgErrorf("go:embed cannot apply to var with multiple names"))
                }
                return
        }
        dest := n.child[0]
        index := dest.findex
        level := dest.level
        rt := n.typ.frameType()

        n.exec = func(f *frame) bltn {
                v, err := embedValue(data, rt)
                if err != nil {
                        panic(n.cfgErrorf("go:embed: %v", err))
                }
                getFrame(f, level).data[index].Set(v)
                return next
        }
}
```

Potential issue: `getExec(n.tnext)` may call setExec on the next node, which is fine. But what if `n.tnext` is nil? For the last var in the chain,
wireChild sets tnext to varNode (the parent). Actually `wireChild` chains child[i-1].tnext = child[i].start and sets last child's tnext = n (the
varNode). The varNode has action aNop and gen nop, so getExec(varNode) → setExec(varNode) → varNode.gen = nop → exec. Fine.

Another issue: `data := n.embed` — for a valueSpec with embed, `n.embed` is set. But in `genEmbedVar`, if n.embed is nil (shouldn't happen), it falls
back to nop. Fine.

Now, `embedValue`:
```go
case reflect.String:
    if len(data.files) != 1 { ... }
    for _, b := range data.files { return reflect.ValueOf(string(b)).Convert(rt), nil }
```
Map iteration returns one entry; since len==1, fine.

For FS:
```go
if rt == reflect.TypeOf(embedfs.FS{}) {
    return reflect.ValueOf(embedfs.New(data.files)), nil
}
```
This works only if the script's `embed.FS` type is exactly `embedfs.FS`. Since we registered it via stdlib, yes. But what if a user uses a different
registration or imports a custom embed package? Not our concern.

Now, potential issue with the `valueSpec` validation in cfg: `n.typ.frameType()` for an unresolved type might be nil or cause panic? For a valueSpec
with a valid type, it should be fine. For embed vars, types are string/[]byte/embed.FS. Good.

Now, let me consider the `parse` change more: `mode := parser.DeclarationErrors | parser.ParseComments`. Previously, `inc` mode added ParseComments.
Now non-inc also has it. But there's a subtle behavior: `parser.ParseFile` with ParseComments returns comments in `f.Comments` and also sets `Doc`
fields on declarations. This is exactly what we need.

But there's a potential issue with `ast.Inspect`: our ast.go's `case *ast.CommentGroup` returns false (skips). With comments now present in AST, the
inspect visits comment groups. It already had `case *ast.CommentGroup: return false`, so no change. Good.

Now, let me think about whether `//go:embed` directives in `_test/*.go` files (the stdlib generic files use embed in actual Go source, not in _test)
could interfere. The `_test` directory has 979 Go files; do any contain `//go:embed`? Only our new embed.go. Good.

hand-written is fine, but there's a concern: `go list std` includes embed; some users may expect `embed` to be exported with the real `embed.FS` type
for use in interpreted code that interacts with the host's embed.FS. But since we can't construct real embed.FS, our custom type is the best we can
do. The feature request doesn't demand the real type.

`embed.FS`, it won't work. But that's an edge case not in the requirements.

Let me think about another important requirement: "Files are resolved relative to the source file's directory using the interpreter's source
filesystem."

In our `resolveEmbed`, we call `collectEmbedEntries(interp.opt.filesystem, dir)`. For `EvalPath("main.go")`, dir = "." (path.Dir("main.go") = ".").
Files in MapFS at "main.go", "hello.txt" — collectEmbedEntries(".") reads entries in ".". Good. For source in subdir "pkg/main.go", dir = "pkg", and
it reads pkg/hello.txt. TestEmbedSubdirSource passed.

Now, what about absolute paths? If EvalPath("/abs/path/main.go"), dir = "/abs/path". realFS can open it. collectEmbedEntries works. But
`path.Join("/abs/path", "hello.txt")` = "/abs/path/hello.txt". Good. For MapFS, keys are relative; an absolute dir wouldn't exist. That's fine — MapFS
is usually used with relative paths.

Now, let's check `collectEmbedEntries` when dir is "." and the FS is MapFS with root files. `fs.ReadDir(fsys, ".")` works. Good.

Now, one more edge: In `resolveEmbed`, when matching a directory pattern, we include all files under it including the directory's own descendants. But
what about the case where a pattern matches a directory that is also a file? Impossible.

Now, let me think about deduplication and "each pattern must match at least one file". Our `matched` is set per-pattern only when `add` actually reads
a file. If a pattern matches a directory with no visible files, matched stays false and we error. But what if a later pattern matches files from the
same directory and includes them; the first pattern still errors. That's correct per Go: each pattern must match at least one file.

Now, what about a pattern matching a file that's already included by a previous pattern? `add` returns early if already in map but still sets `matched
= true`? Let me check:
```go
add := func(rel string) error {
    if _, ok := data.files[rel]; ok {
        return nil
    }
    ...
    data.files[rel] = b
    matched = true
    return nil
}
```
If already present, it returns nil WITHOUT setting matched. So if a pattern's only match is a file already added by a previous pattern, `matched`
stays false and we'd error "pattern matched no files". In Go, is that an error? Example:
```go
//go:embed data
//go:embed data/a.txt
var f embed.FS
```
Both patterns match data/a.txt. The second pattern's match is already present. Does Go consider it as matching? The Go tool counts each pattern's
matches independently; even if the file is already matched, the pattern still matches the file, so it should not error. Our implementation would error
on the second pattern because `add` early-returns before setting matched.

Let me fix: in `add`, set `matched = true` even if already present, or check match separately. Better: set matched=true before the early return:
```go
add := func(rel string) error {
    if _, ok := data.files[rel]; !ok {
        full := ...
        b, err := ...
        data.files[rel] = b
    }
    matched = true
    return nil
}
```
Let me fix this.

Also, for a pattern matching a directory where all files were already added by a prior pattern, `add` for each file returns early but matched stays
false. With the fix, matched=true for each file added attempt. Good.

Let me update resolveEmbed.

func TestEmbedOverlappingPatterns(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	"embed"
	"fmt"
)

	fmt.Println(string(b))
}
`)},

	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if out != "A\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", out)
	}
}

func TestEmbedEval(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"hello.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("eval data")},
	}
	var stdout bytes.Buffer
	i := interp.New(interp.Options{SourcecodeFilesystem: fsys, Stdout: &stdout})
	if err := i.Use(stdlib.Symbols); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	_, err := i.Eval(`package main
import (
	_ "embed"
	"fmt"
)
//go:embed hello.txt
var s string
func main() { fmt.Println(s) }
`)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if stdout.String() != "eval data\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", stdout.String())
	}
}

Let me also think about the `parseEmbedPatterns` function. There's a potential bug with backslash escapes in double-quoted patterns: when scanning for
the end quote, we skip `\\` as escape (end += 2). But what about `\"` inside? `s[end] == '\\'` handles backslash followed by any char, including `\"`.
Good. Then `strconv.Unquote` handles it.

Now, one concern: `strconv.Unquote` on a back-quoted string `` ` `` doesn't process escapes and can't contain backticks. Our scanner for backquote:
`if s[0] == '"' && s[end] == '\\'` only skips escapes for double-quoted; for backquote, `s[end] == s[0]` breaks at first backtick. Good.

Now, let me think about the "all:" prefix validation. `splitAll` strips "all:" from the beginning. But what if the pattern is literally `all:` (empty
after)? validateEmbedPattern("") returns error. Good.

What if a pattern is `all:dir` and `path.Match("dir", ...)`. Good.

Now, let me consider `validateEmbedPattern` — it rejects patterns containing `\`, but path.Match uses `/` separator and treats `\` as normal char.
Go's embed rejects backslash. Good.

Now, let me consider the requirement "Files starting with . or _ are excluded unless the all: prefix is used." Our `visible` function excludes any
file whose path has a hidden component. But what about a file named `_test.go` at top level with pattern `*.go`? It would be excluded. That matches Go
(files starting with _ excluded). Good.

Now, let me think about a pattern that directly names a hidden file with `all:`:
`//go:embed all:.hidden` — all=true, pattern ".hidden", path.Match(".hidden", ".hidden") true, included. Good.

Now, let me consider whether `collectEmbedEntries` should include directories that are empty. In Go, "Matches for empty directories are ignored." Our
collect includes dirs regardless of whether they have files. When matching a dir pattern, if the dir has no files, `add` never called, matched false →
error. That's correct per "matches for empty directories are ignored" + "each pattern must match at least one file or non-empty directory". Good.

Now, one more edge: pattern `*` with a directory that contains hidden files only. Go: `*` matches the directory (non-empty? if it has hidden files, is
it non-empty? In Go, hidden files are excluded, so the directory effectively has no embedded files → "matches for empty directories ignored" → pattern
may match no files → error). Our implementation: dir matches, but `visible` excludes all files, so add never called, matched false → error. Correct.

Now let me think about the embed.FS ReadDir behavior for `data` with a subdirectory. TestEmbedFS expected 3 entries (a.txt, b.txt, sub). That's
correct. ReadDir entries sorted: a.txt, b.txt, sub (alphabetical). Good.
