package interp

import (
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"go/ast"
	"go/token"
	"io/fs"
	"path"

	"github.com/traefik/yaegi/internal/embedfs"
	"reflect"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
)

// embedData stores the resolved files of a //go:embed directive.
type embedData struct {
	dir      string
	patterns []string
	files    map[string][]byte
}

// embedPatterns extracts the //go:embed directives from a comment group.
// It returns the list of patterns found in all the embed lines.
func embedPatterns(cg *ast.CommentGroup) ([]string, error) {
	if cg == nil {
		return nil, nil
	}
	var patterns []string
	for _, c := range cg.List {
		text := c.Text
		if len(text) < len("//go:embed") || text[:len("//go:embed")] != "//go:embed" {
			continue
		}
		rest := text[len("//go:embed"):]
		if rest != "" && rest[0] != ' ' && rest[0] != '\t' {
			// Not a go:embed directive, just a comment starting with go:embed.
			continue
		}
		ps, err := parseEmbedPatterns(rest)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
		patterns = append(patterns, ps...)
	}
	return patterns, nil
}

// parseEmbedPatterns splits a go:embed line into its patterns. Quoted
// patterns are supported as in the Go specification.
func parseEmbedPatterns(s string) ([]string, error) {
	s = strings.TrimSpace(s)
	if s == "" {
		return nil, errors.New("go:embed: empty pattern")
	}
	var patterns []string
	for len(s) > 0 {
		switch s[0] {
		case '"', '`':
			// Find the end of the quoted string.
			end := 1
			for end < len(s) {
				if s[0] == '"' && s[end] == '\\' {
					end += 2
					continue
				}
				if s[end] == s[0] {
					break
				}
				end++
			}
			if end >= len(s) {
				return nil, errors.New("go:embed: unterminated quoted pattern")
			}
			p, err := strconv.Unquote(s[:end+1])
			if err != nil {
				return nil, err
			}
			patterns = append(patterns, p)
			s = strings.TrimSpace(s[end+1:])
		default:
			end := strings.IndexAny(s, " \t\r\n")
			if end < 0 {
				end = len(s)
			}
			patterns = append(patterns, s[:end])
			s = strings.TrimSpace(s[end:])
		}
	}
	return patterns, nil
}

// splitAll returns the pattern without the "all:" prefix and a boolean
// indicating whether the prefix was present.
func splitAll(pattern string) (string, bool) {
	if strings.HasPrefix(pattern, "all:") {
		return pattern[4:], true
	}
	return pattern, false
}

// validateEmbedPattern checks that a pattern is valid for use in a
// //go:embed directive.
func validateEmbedPattern(pattern string) error {
	if pattern == "" {
		return errors.New("go:embed: empty pattern")
	}
	if pattern == "." || pattern == ".." {
		return fmt.Errorf("go:embed: invalid pattern %q", pattern)
	}
	if strings.HasPrefix(pattern, "/") || strings.HasSuffix(pattern, "/") {
		return fmt.Errorf("go:embed: invalid pattern %q", pattern)
	}
	if strings.Contains(pattern, "\\") {
		return fmt.Errorf("go:embed: invalid pattern %q", pattern)
	}
	for _, part := range strings.Split(pattern, "/") {
		if part == "" || part == "." || part == ".." {
			return fmt.Errorf("go:embed: invalid pattern %q", pattern)
		}
	}
	if _, err := path.Match(pattern, "dummy"); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("go:embed: invalid pattern %q: %v", pattern, err)
	}
	return nil
}

// visible reports whether a relative path contains no hidden component
// (a component beginning with '.' or '_').
func visible(name string) bool {
	for _, part := range strings.Split(name, "/") {
		if strings.HasPrefix(part, ".") || strings.HasPrefix(part, "_") {
			return false
		}
	}
	return true
}

// collectEmbedEntries walks the directory dir and returns the relative paths
// of all directories and regular files it contains.
func collectEmbedEntries(fsys fs.FS, dir string) (dirs, files map[string]bool, err error) {
	dirs = map[string]bool{}
	files = map[string]bool{}
	var walk func(root, rel string) error
	walk = func(root, rel string) error {
		entries, err := fs.ReadDir(fsys, root)
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		for _, e := range entries {
			childRel := e.Name()
			if rel != "" {
				childRel = path.Join(rel, e.Name())
			}
			childRoot := path.Join(root, e.Name())
			if e.IsDir() {
				dirs[childRel] = true
				if err := walk(childRoot, childRel); err != nil {
					return err
				}
			} else {
				files[childRel] = true
			}
		}
		return nil
	}
	if err := walk(dir, ""); err != nil {
		return nil, nil, err
	}
	return dirs, files, nil
}

// resolveEmbed resolves the patterns against the source directory dir and
// reads the matching files from the interpreter source filesystem.
func (interp *Interpreter) resolveEmbed(dir string, patterns []string) (*embedData, error) {
	dirs, files, err := collectEmbedEntries(interp.opt.filesystem, dir)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}

	data := &embedData{dir: dir, patterns: patterns, files: map[string][]byte{}}

	for _, raw := range patterns {
		pattern, all := splitAll(raw)
		if err := validateEmbedPattern(pattern); err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
		matched := false
		add := func(rel string) error {
			if _, ok := data.files[rel]; !ok {
				full := path.Join(dir, rel)
				b, err := fs.ReadFile(interp.opt.filesystem, full)
				if err != nil {
					return err
				}
				data.files[rel] = b
			}
			matched = true
			return nil
		}

		// Match directories: embed the whole subtree.
		for d := range dirs {
			ok, err := path.Match(pattern, d)
			if err != nil {
				return nil, err
			}
			if !ok {
				continue
			}
			prefix := d + "/"
			for f := range files {
				if f == d || strings.HasPrefix(f, prefix) {
					if all || visible(f) {
						if err := add(f); err != nil {
							return nil, err
						}
					}
				}
			}
		}

		// Match files.
		for f := range files {
			ok, err := path.Match(pattern, f)
			if err != nil {
				return nil, err
			}
			if !ok {
				continue
			}
			if all || visible(f) {
				if err := add(f); err != nil {
					return nil, err
				}
			}
		}

		if !matched {
			return nil, fmt.Errorf("go:embed: pattern %q matched no files", raw)
		}
	}

	return data, nil
}

// genEmbedVar generates the initialization of a global variable declared with
// a //go:embed directive.
func genEmbedVar(n *node) {
	next := getExec(n.tnext)
	data := n.embed
	if data == nil {
		n.gen = nop
		n.gen(n)
		return
	}

	// The embedded declaration must declare a single variable.
	if len(n.child) < 2 || n.nleft != 1 {
		n.exec = func(f *frame) bltn {
			panic(n.cfgErrorf("go:embed cannot apply to var with multiple names"))
		}
		return
	}
	dest := n.child[0]
	index := dest.findex
	level := dest.level
	rt := n.typ.frameType()

	n.exec = func(f *frame) bltn {
		v, err := embedValue(data, rt)
		if err != nil {
			panic(n.cfgErrorf("go:embed: %v", err))
		}
		getFrame(f, level).data[index].Set(v)
		return next
	}
}

// embedValue builds the reflect.Value of the embedded content for the given
// target type.
func embedValue(data *embedData, rt reflect.Type) (reflect.Value, error) {
	switch rt.Kind() {
	case reflect.String:
		if len(data.files) != 1 {
			return reflect.Value{}, fmt.Errorf("pattern matches %d files, but must match exactly one file for a string", len(data.files))
		}
		for _, b := range data.files {
			return reflect.ValueOf(string(b)).Convert(rt), nil
		}
	case reflect.Slice:
		if rt.Elem().Kind() != reflect.Uint8 {
			return reflect.Value{}, fmt.Errorf("go:embed cannot apply to var of type %s", rt)
		}
		if len(data.files) != 1 {
			return reflect.Value{}, fmt.Errorf("pattern matches %d files, but must match exactly one file for a []byte", len(data.files))
		}
		for _, b := range data.files {
			return reflect.ValueOf(b).Convert(rt), nil
		}
	}

	if rt == reflect.TypeOf(embedfs.FS{}) {
		return reflect.ValueOf(embedfs.New(data.files)), nil
	}

	return reflect.Value{}, fmt.Errorf("go:embed cannot apply to var of type %s", rt)
}

// embedDirFor returns the directory of the source file associated with the
// given position.
func (interp *Interpreter) embedDirFor(pos token.Pos) string {
	filename := interp.fset.Position(pos).Filename
	if filename == "" {
		return "."
	}
	return path.Dir(filename)
}

package interp_test

import (
	"bytes"
	"io/fs"
	"testing"
	"testing/fstest"

	"github.com/traefik/yaegi/interp"
	"github.com/traefik/yaegi/stdlib"
)

func runEmbed(t *testing.T, fsys fs.FS, src string) (string, error) {
	t.Helper()
	var stdout bytes.Buffer
	i := interp.New(interp.Options{SourcecodeFilesystem: fsys, Stdout: &stdout})
	if err := i.Use(stdlib.Symbols); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	_, err := i.EvalPath("main.go")
	return stdout.String(), err
}

func TestEmbedString(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	_ "embed"
	"fmt"
)
//go:embed hello.txt
var s string
func main() { fmt.Println(s) }
`)},

/*
Package interp provides a complete Go interpreter.

For the Go language itself, refer to the official Go specification
https://golang.org/ref/spec.

# Importing packages

Packages can be imported in source or binary form, using the standard
Go import statement. In source form, packages are searched first in the
vendor directory, the preferred way to store source dependencies. If not
found in vendor, sources modules will be searched in GOPATH. Go modules
are not supported yet by yaegi.

Binary form packages are compiled and linked with the interpreter
executable, and exposed to scripts with the Use method. The extract
subcommand of yaegi can be used to generate package wrappers.

# Custom build tags

Custom build tags allow to control which files in imported source
packages are interpreted, in the same way as the "-tags" option of the
"go build" command. Setting a custom build tag spans globally for all
future imports of the session.

A build tag is a line comment that begins

	// yaegi:tags

that lists the build constraints to be satisfied by the further
imports of source packages.

For example the following custom build tag

	// yaegi:tags noasm

Will ensure that an import of a package will exclude files containing

	// +build !noasm

And include files containing

	// +build noasm
*/
package interp

// BUG(marc): Support for recursive types is incomplete.
// BUG(marc): Support of types implementing multiple interfaces is incomplete.

resolves embed at CompileAST time. Good. But what if the user uses `Compile` (string) and then `Execute`? compileSrc sets interp.name to
DefaultSourceName, dir ".". Good.

remains the original input. In `ast.go`, `embedDirFor(pos)` uses fset position filename = `name` (the per-file path). Good. So embed resolution for

for _, child := range n.child {

"cannot apply to local variable". But in a function body, the GenDecl's parent is a blockStmt, not fileStmt. So anc.node.anc.kind == blockStmt, not
fileStmt → error. Good.

But wait, for a package-level var, `anc.node.anc.kind` is fileStmt. For a var inside a func, `anc.node.anc` is blockStmt (or declStmt). Let me verify
the anc chain: in ast.go, a `var` inside a function body appears as `*ast.DeclStmt` wrapping `*ast.GenDecl`? Actually Go AST: inside a function, `var
x int` is a `DeclStmt` containing a `GenDecl`. The ast.go `case *ast.DeclStmt` creates a declStmt node, and then its child GenDecl has anc = declStmt
node. So for a local var, `anc.node.anc.kind` is declStmt, not fileStmt. Our check `!= fileStmt` catches it. Good.

cd /app && cat >> interp/embed_test.go <<'EOF'

func TestEmbedLocalVarError(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import _ "embed"
func main() {
	//go:embed a.txt
	var s string
	_ = s
}
`)},
		"a.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("A")},
	}
	_, err := runEmbed(t, fsys)
	if err == nil || !bytes.Contains([]byte(err.Error()), []byte("local variable")) {
		t.Fatalf("got err %v, want local variable error", err)
	}
}

func TestEmbedGroupMultipleError(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import _ "embed"
//go:embed a.txt
var (
	x string
	y string
)
func main() { _ = x; _ = y }
`)},
		"a.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("A")},
	}
	_, err := runEmbed(t, fsys)
	if err == nil || !bytes.Contains([]byte(err.Error()), []byte("group of variables")) {
		t.Fatalf("got err %v, want group error", err)
	}
}
EOF
gofmt -w interp/embed_test.go && go test ./interp -run 'TestEmbedLocal|TestEmbedGroupMultiple' -v 2>&1 | tail -20

Still running. Let me continue with review.

Let me examine the `internal/embedfs` package more carefully for correctness and any potential issue with the interpreter's binary method dispatch.

When `content.ReadFile("a.txt")` is called in interpreted code, yaegi resolves the method `ReadFile` on the binary type `embedfs.FS`. Since
`embedfs.FS` is a value type with method `ReadFile(name string) ([]byte, error)`, and we registered the type via
`reflect.ValueOf((*embedfs.FS)(nil))`, the interpreter should find the method via reflection. TestEmbedFS confirmed direct `content.ReadFile` works.

But what about `content.Open("data")` returning an `*embedDir` which implements `fs.ReadDirFile`? The interpreted code type-asserts
`d.(fs.ReadDirFile)`. The `embedDir` type is unexported (`*embedDir`), but it's returned as `fs.File` interface value. When type-asserting to
`fs.ReadDirFile`, yaegi does a dynamic type check. Since `*embedDir` implements `fs.ReadDirFile`, the assertion should succeed. TestEmbedReadDirFile
passed. Good.

Now, let me think about a potential issue: `embedfs.FS` has an unexported field `files map[string][]byte`. When the interpreted code does `var f
embed.FS` and we set it, the value is our `embedfs.FS`. If interpreted code tries to compare `f == (embed.FS{})`, it would compare the map field (maps
are not comparable), causing a runtime panic. Real embed.FS is also not comparable? Actually embed.FS contains a pointer field, so it IS comparable
(pointer comparison). But our struct has a map field, making it non-comparable. This could be a subtle incompatibility. For example:
```go
var f embed.FS
if f == (embed.FS{}) { ... }
```
In real Go, `embed.FS{} == embed.FS{}` is true (both nil pointer). In our implementation, comparing two embedfs.FS values would panic because map
fields aren't comparable.

To be more faithful, we could make FS comparable by using a pointer to a struct holding the map:
```go
type FS struct {
    files *filesData
}
type filesData struct { m map[string][]byte }
```
Then FS is comparable (pointer comparison). But that changes the internal layout. Let me consider if this matters for the feature request. The
requirement says "read-only filesystem" and implements the interfaces. It doesn't mention comparability. But to avoid surprises, making it comparable
is nicer. Also, `fs.FS` interface values with FS (value) would be comparable? Not important.

Let me change `embedfs.FS` to hold a pointer to the map (or to a struct). E.g.:
```go
type FS struct {
    files map[string][]byte
}
```
becomes
```go
type FS struct {
    files *fileMap
}
type fileMap struct { m map[string][]byte }
```
Then `lookup` and `readDir` access `f.files.m`. `New` creates `&fileMap{m: files}`.

But wait, `reflect.TypeOf(embedfs.FS{})` is still the same type. The methods stay on value receiver. The struct is comparable (pointer field). This is
more faithful to real embed.FS (which has a pointer field `files *[]file`).

Let me make this change. It's a small refactor.

Actually, do we need FS to be comparable? The interpreter's value assignment just stores the value. If interpreted code compares, it'd panic with our
current map field. Let me fix it to be robust.

Let me update internal/embedfs/embedfs.go.

// Package embedfs provides a read-only in-memory filesystem used to back
// //go:embed directives in the interpreter.
package embedfs

import (
	"errors"
	"io"
	"io/fs"
	"path"
	"sort"
	"strings"
	"time"
)

// FS is a read-only in-memory filesystem built from //go:embed directives.
// It implements fs.FS, fs.ReadFileFS and fs.ReadDirFS.
type FS struct {
	files *fileMap
}

// fileMap holds the contents of the filesystem. It is kept behind a pointer
// so that FS values remain comparable, like the standard embed.FS type.
type fileMap struct {
	files map[string][]byte
}

var (
	_ fs.FS         = (*FS)(nil)
	_ fs.ReadFileFS = (*FS)(nil)
	_ fs.ReadDirFS  = (*FS)(nil)
)

// New returns a read-only filesystem containing the given files.
// The keys of files are slash-separated paths relative to the filesystem root.
func New(files map[string][]byte) FS {
	return FS{files: &fileMap{files: files}}
}

// Open opens the named file or directory for reading.
func (f FS) Open(name string) (fs.File, error) {
	if name == "" {
		name = "."
	}
	if !fs.ValidPath(name) && name != "." {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "open", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrInvalid}
	}
	name = path.Clean(name)
	isDir, ok := f.lookup(name)
	if !ok {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "open", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrNotExist}
	}
	if isDir {
		return &embedDir{name: name, entries: f.readDir(name)}, nil
	}
	return &embedFile{name: name, data: append([]byte(nil), f.files.files[name]...)}, nil
}

// ReadFile reads the named file and returns its contents. A fresh copy is
// returned on every call.
func (f FS) ReadFile(name string) ([]byte, error) {
	if !fs.ValidPath(name) {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrInvalid}
	}
	name = path.Clean(name)
	isDir, ok := f.lookup(name)
	if !ok {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrNotExist}
	}
	if isDir {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: errors.New("is a directory")}
	}
	return append([]byte(nil), f.files.files[name]...), nil
}

// ReadDir reads the named directory and returns a list of directory entries
// sorted by filename.
func (f FS) ReadDir(name string) ([]fs.DirEntry, error) {
	if name == "" {
		name = "."
	}
	if !fs.ValidPath(name) && name != "." {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrInvalid}
	}
	name = path.Clean(name)
	isDir, ok := f.lookup(name)
	if !ok {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: fs.ErrNotExist}
	}
	if !isDir {
		return nil, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: name, Err: errors.New("not a directory")}
	}
	return f.readDir(name), nil
}

func (f FS) lookup(name string) (isDir, ok bool) {
	if f.files == nil || f.files.files == nil {
		return false, false
	}
	name = strings.TrimSuffix(name, "/")
	if name == "." {
		return true, len(f.files.files) > 0
	}
	if _, ok := f.files.files[name]; ok {
		return false, true
	}
	prefix := name + "/"
	for p := range f.files.files {
		if strings.HasPrefix(p, prefix) {
			return true, true
		}
	}
	return false, false
}

func (f FS) readDir(name string) []fs.DirEntry {
	prefix := ""
	if name != "." {
		prefix = name + "/"
	}
	type entry struct {
		base  string
		isDir bool
	}
	m := map[string]entry{}
	for p := range f.files.files {
		if !strings.HasPrefix(p, prefix) {
			continue
		}
		rest := p[len(prefix):]
		if rest == "" {
			continue
		}
		base, sub, _ := strings.Cut(rest, "/")
		e, ok := m[base]
		if !ok {
			e = entry{base: base}
		}
		if sub != "" {
			e.isDir = true
		}
		m[base] = e
	}
	names := make([]string, 0, len(m))
	for base := range m {
		names = append(names, base)
	}
	sort.Strings(names)
	entries := make([]fs.DirEntry, len(names))
	for i, base := range names {
		e := m[base]
		entries[i] = &embedDirEntry{name: e.base, isDir: e.isDir, size: 0}
	}
	return entries
}

type embedFile struct {
	name   string
	data   []byte
	offset int64
}

func (f *embedFile) Close() error { return nil }

func (f *embedFile) Read(p []byte) (int, error) {
	if f.offset >= int64(len(f.data)) {
		return 0, io.EOF
	}
	n := copy(p, f.data[f.offset:])
	f.offset += int64(n)
	return n, nil
}

func (f *embedFile) Stat() (fs.FileInfo, error) {
	return &embedFileInfo{name: f.name, size: int64(len(f.data))}, nil
}

func (f *embedFile) Seek(offset int64, whence int) (int64, error) {
	switch whence {
	case io.SeekStart:
		// offset unchanged
	case io.SeekCurrent:
		offset += f.offset
	case io.SeekEnd:
		offset += int64(len(f.data))
	default:
		return 0, &fs.PathError{Op: "seek", Path: f.name, Err: fs.ErrInvalid}
	}
	if offset < 0 || offset > int64(len(f.data)) {
		return 0, &fs.PathError{Op: "seek", Path: f.name, Err: fs.ErrInvalid}
	}
	f.offset = offset
	return offset, nil
}

type embedDir struct {
	name    string
	entries []fs.DirEntry
	offset  int
}

func (d *embedDir) Close() error { return nil }

func (d *embedDir) Read([]byte) (int, error) {
	return 0, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: d.name, Err: errors.New("is a directory")}
}

func (d *embedDir) Stat() (fs.FileInfo, error) {
	return &embedFileInfo{name: d.name, isDir: true}, nil
}

func (d *embedDir) ReadDir(n int) ([]fs.DirEntry, error) {
	if n <= 0 {
		d.offset = 0
		res := make([]fs.DirEntry, len(d.entries))
		copy(res, d.entries)
		return res, nil
	}
	if d.offset >= len(d.entries) {
		return nil, io.EOF
	}

func TestEmbedQuotedPattern(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	_ "embed"
	"fmt"
)
//go:embed "hello world.txt"
var s string
func main() { fmt.Println(s) }
`)},
		"hello world.txt": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("spaced")},
	}
	out, err := runEmbed(t, fsys)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if out != "spaced\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", out)
	}
}

STEP 168 SOURCE agent
REASONING_CONTENT
Still running. Let me continue reviewing potential edge cases.

One important thing: In `resolveEmbed`, we call `collectEmbedEntries` which reads the whole directory tree. For a MapFS, `fs.ReadDir` on a directory
with no explicit directory entry works if there are files under it. But what about an empty directory? MapFS can't represent empty dirs (except via
explicit dir entry?). `fstest.MapFS` supports directory entries as files with Mode().IsDir(). If an empty dir exists, `collectEmbedEntries` would
include it in dirs, and a pattern matching it would have no files → error. Go would ignore empty dir matches. Our behavior errors, which matches
"patterns matching no files produce an error". Good.

Now, let me think about the `visible` function for paths like `data/` — we never pass trailing slashes. Good.

Now, let me consider `path.Join(dir, rel)` when dir is "." and rel is "a.txt" → "a.txt". Good. When dir is "pkg" and rel is "a.txt" → "pkg/a.txt".
Good.

Now, let me consider `fs.ReadFile(interp.opt.filesystem, full)` where full is an absolute path and filesystem is realFS. realFS.Open passes to os.Open
which handles absolute paths. Good.

Now, let me think about whether `collectEmbedEntries` should skip the source file itself. The source file `main.go` is in the same directory as
embedded files. If a pattern `*` is used, it would embed `main.go` too. In real Go, `//go:embed *` would indeed embed all files including the .go
source file? Actually Go's embed excludes the source file? No — `//go:embed *` embeds all files in the directory including .go files? I believe Go
does embed .go files (it doesn't exclude them; it only excludes hidden and _ files, and skips files with special punctuation). So embedding main.go is
allowed. Our implementation includes it. Fine.

Now, let me think about the `all:` prefix with a pattern that directly names a file, e.g. `all:.hidden` — our code handles it.

Now, let me consider a potential bug in `parseEmbedPatterns`: if the line has a quoted pattern followed immediately by a non-space char, e.g.
`"a.txt"b.txt`, our code would parse `"a.txt"` then `b.txt`. That's more permissive than Go but not harmful.

Now, let me consider the error message for invalid pattern: `validateEmbedPattern` uses `path.Match(pattern, "dummy")` to check syntax. But
`path.Match` with a pattern containing `[` without closing returns error. Good.

func TestEmbedHiddenExcluded(t *testing.T) {
	fsys := fstest.MapFS{
		"main.go": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte(`package main
import (
	"embed"
	"fmt"
	"io/fs"
)
//go:embed data
var f embed.FS
func main() {
	entries, _ := fs.ReadDir(f, "data")
	fmt.Println(len(entries))
	_, err := f.ReadFile("data/.hidden")
	if err == nil { fmt.Println("hidden included") } else { fmt.Println("hidden excluded") }
}
`)},
		"data/a.txt":   &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("A")},
		"data/.hidden": &fstest.MapFile{Data: []byte("H")},
	}
	out, err := runEmbed(t, fsys)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if out != "1\nhidden excluded\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", out)
	}
}
EOF

Good.

Now let me think about whether there are any remaining gaps in the implementation:

1. `//go:embed` with `string`/`[]byte` and a directory pattern that contains exactly one file. Our compile-time check allows it (len(files)==1). In
real Go, is a directory pattern allowed for string? Yes, if it matches exactly one file. E.g. `//go:embed dir` where dir has one file. Our
implementation supports it. Let me add a test? It's covered by the logic; not necessary.

2. `embed.FS` with a single file. Works.

3. `//go:embed` patterns with `all:` for string — works.

4. Multiple `//go:embed` lines before one var combining patterns — tested with embed.FS (TestEmbedMultipleLines). Also should work for string? In real
Go, string can have only one pattern total? Actually Go says "The //go:embed line for a variable of type string or []byte can have only a single
pattern, and that pattern can match only a single file." Multiple lines with one pattern each = multiple patterns, which is not allowed for string.
Our implementation: for string, len(data.files)==1 check allows multiple patterns if they collectively match one file. Real Go would reject multiple
patterns for string. Should we enforce? The feature request says "For string and []byte, patterns must resolve to exactly one file." It doesn't say
only one pattern. So our behavior (allowing multiple patterns that resolve to one file) satisfies the requirement. Fine.

Now, let me think about a potential issue with the `embed` package registration and `go generate`. If someone runs `go generate ./stdlib`, it
regenerates stdlib files but won't touch our hand-written `stdlib/embed.go`. Good.

					return false
				}
				if len(patterns) == 0 && anc.node.embed != nil && len(anc.node.embed.patterns) > 0 {
					patterns = anc.node.embed.patterns
					anc.node.embed.patterns = nil
				}
				if len(patterns) > 0 {
					if anc.node.anc.kind != fileStmt {
						err = astError(fmt.Errorf("go:embed cannot apply to a local variable"))
						return false
					}
					if len(a.Names) > 1 {
						err = astError(fmt.Errorf("go:embed cannot apply to var with multiple names"))
						return false
					}
					if a.Values != nil {
						err = astError(fmt.Errorf("go:embed cannot apply to var with initializer"))
						return false
					}
					dir := interp.embedDirFor(pos)
					var data *embedData
					data, err = interp.resolveEmbed(dir, patterns)
					if err != nil {
						err = astError(err)
						return false
					}
					n.embed = data
				}
			}
			st.push(n, nod)

		default:
			err = astError(fmt.Errorf("ast: %T not implemented, line %s", a, interp.fset.Position(pos)))
			return false
		}
		return true
	})


OTHER STEP FIELDS

to a preceding declaration. For example:
```go
//go:embed a.txt
import "fmt"

var s string
```

directive" and an error. Our implementation would silently ignore it, and s would be empty. That's a minor incompatibility (more permissive). The
feature request doesn't require detecting misplaced directives. Acceptable.

Now let me think about whether there's anything else to address from the feature request:

1. ✅ Support `//go:embed` directives for package-level vars, standalone and grouped.
2. ✅ Target types: string, []byte, embed.FS.
3. ✅ Exactly one file for string/[]byte.
4. ✅ Patterns: space-separated, path.Match, multiple lines combine.
5. ✅ Directory pattern embeds tree.
6. ✅ Hidden files excluded unless all:.
7. ✅ No files → error.
8. ✅ embed.FS implements fs.FS, fs.ReadFileFS, fs.ReadDirFS; ReadDir sorted; dirs implement fs.ReadDirFile; ReadFile returns independent copy.
9. ✅ Variable holds content before first statement; standard init doesn't overwrite.

Let me also double-check the "not overwritten" requirement once more with a scenario where standard init would reset: our TestEmbedNotOverwritten has
`var copy = s`, and both print AA. Good.

Now, one more subtle thing: in `genGlobalVarDecl`, we set `n.gen = genEmbedVar` before computing dependencies. But `getVarDependencies` walks the node
and looks at identExprs. For an embedded valueSpec, the children are [ident, type]. The type node might contain an identExpr for the package `embed`?
For `embed.FS` type, the child is a selectorExpr (embed.FS). `getVarDependencies` walks and for selectorExpr's child[1] (ident "FS"), it checks
`n.anc.kind == selectorExpr && childPos(n) == 1` and returns false. So no dependency on embed.FS. Good. For string/[]byte, the type child is identExpr
"string"/"[]byte"? Actually `[]byte` is an arrayType node, `string` is identExpr. `getVarDependencies` sees identExpr "string" and looks up scope —
finds typeSym, not varSym, so no dependency. Good.

Now, let me also consider: if an embed var is used in another var's initializer before it's initialized (dependency ordering), our genEmbedVar is in
the chain in the correct order because genGlobalVarDecl's dependency sort includes it. TestEmbedNotOverwritten verified `var copy = s` after s works.
Good.

Now, let me consider whether the `embed` package should also be added to `stdlib`'s `go list std` comment. Not needed.

Let me check `git status` and then commit.

But first, let me reconsider one design decision: the `embedData` resolution happens during `ast()` (AST conversion), which is called from both
`compileSrc` and `importSrc`. In `importSrc`, `interp.opt.filesystem` is available. Good.

However, there's a potential issue: `ast()` is also called from `CompileAST`, which may be used with a custom AST. If the AST was parsed with a
different FileSet (not interp.fset), then `interp.fset.Position(pos).Filename` might be wrong or empty. But the docs for CompileAST say "WARNING: The
node must have been parsed using interp.FileSet()." So users should use interp.fset. Good.

Now, let me consider whether `parse` with `inc=true` and comments always parsed could cause the `wrapInMain` path to attach comments incorrectly. In
REPL, when the first token is not package/decl, src is wrapped in `func main() { ... }`. If a user types `//go:embed hello.txt` alone (just a
comment), firstToken would return... the scanner would skip the comment and then hit EOF, returning token.EOF. The switch default would wrap it in
main, and then the comment becomes a comment inside the function body with no var following — no embed processing. Fine.

Now, let me look at the `other_tests.log` completion. It's done. All good.

	name    string
	entries []fs.DirEntry
	offset  int

func (d *embedDir) Close() error { return nil }

func (d *embedDir) Read([]byte) (int, error) {
	return 0, &fs.PathError{Op: "read", Path: d.name, Err: errors.New("is a directory")}
}

func (d *embedDir) Stat() (fs.FileInfo, error) {
	return &embedFileInfo{name: d.name, isDir: true}, nil
}

func (d *embedDir) ReadDir(n int) ([]fs.DirEntry, error) {
	if n <= 0 {
		d.offset = 0
		res := make([]fs.DirEntry, len(d.entries))
		copy(res, d.entries)
		return res, nil
	}
	if d.offset >= len(d.entries) {
		return nil, io.EOF
	}
	res := d.entries[d.offset:]
	if n < len(res) {
		res = res[:n]
	}
	d.offset += len(res)
	return res, nil
}

type embedDirEntry struct {
	name  string
	isDir bool
	size  int64
}

func (e *embedDirEntry) Name() string { return e.name }
func (e *embedDirEntry) IsDir() bool  { return e.isDir }
func (e *embedDirEntry) Type() fs.FileMode {
	if e.isDir {
		return fs.ModeDir
	}
	return 0
}
func (e *embedDirEntry) Info() (fs.FileInfo, error) {
	return &embedFileInfo{name: e.name, isDir: e.isDir, size: e.size}, nil
}

type embedFileInfo struct {
	name  string
	isDir bool
	size  int64
}

func (i *embedFileInfo) Name() string { return i.name }
func (i *embedFileInfo) Size() int64  { return i.size }
func (i *embedFileInfo) Mode() fs.FileMode {
	if i.isDir {
		return fs.ModeDir
	}
	return 0
}
func (i *embedFileInfo) ModTime() time.Time { return time.Time{} }
func (i *embedFileInfo) IsDir() bool        { return i.isDir }
func (i *embedFileInfo) Sys() interface{}   { return nil }

cd /app && git commit -m "interp: support //go:embed directives

Implement //go:embed support for package-level variables of type string,
[]byte and embed.FS. Patterns are resolved relative to the source file
directory using the interpreter source filesystem, with path.Match glob
semantics, directory tree embedding, and hidden file exclusion unless the
all: prefix is used. Embedded values are initialized before the first
interpreted statement executes and are not overwritten by standard
variable initialization.

The embed.FS target is backed by a new internal read-only filesystem that
implements fs.FS, fs.ReadFileFS and fs.ReadDirFS, with sorted ReadDir
entries, fs.ReadDirFile directories and independent ReadFile copies. The
embed package is registered through stdlib.Symbols.

Add unit tests, an integration test in _test, and documentation." 2>&1 | tail -10

commit 219eaa70d13328040750b017030fe02e0cecfc67
Author: Yaegi Dev <dev@yaegi.local>
Date:   Thu Aug 6 05:18:36 2026 +0000

    interp: support //go:embed directives
    
    Implement //go:embed support for package-level variables of type string,
    []byte and embed.FS. Patterns are resolved relative to the source file
    directory using the interpreter source filesystem, with path.Match glob
    semantics, directory tree embedding, and hidden file exclusion unless the
    all: prefix is used. Embedded values are initialized before the first
    interpreted statement executes and are not overwritten by standard
    variable initialization.
    
    The embed.FS target is backed by a new internal read-only filesystem that
    implements fs.FS, fs.ReadFileFS and fs.ReadDirFS, with sorted ReadDir
    entries, fs.ReadDirFile directories and independent ReadFile copies. The
    embed package is registered through stdlib.Symbols.
    
    Add unit tests, an integration test in _test, and documentation.

Actually, let me reconsider the `embedDirFor` function. It uses `interp.fset.Position(pos).Filename`. For a `pos` from the AST, `interp.fset.Position`
returns the filename. But what if the same filename has been added multiple times to the fset (e.g., buildOk adds a file with PackageClauseOnly, then
parse adds the full file)? The fset may have two entries with the same name; `Position` returns the position in the last added file with that base.
That's fine — both have the same filename.

Now, let me also think about `parseEmbedPatterns` with a line like `//go:embed a.txt b.txt` — splits into two. Good.

STEP 225 SOURCE agent
REASONING_CONTENT
Still running. Let me continue waiting. The test should finish in ~2 more minutes. Let me do a quick check of the README to see if it mentions embed
support (not needed).

Actually, let me think about one more potential issue: the `embedfs.FS` methods are exported but the type is in `internal/embedfs`. When `stdlib`
registers it via `reflect.ValueOf((*embedfs.FS)(nil))`, the interpreter gets a reflect.Type whose PkgPath is
`github.com/traefik/yaegi/internal/embedfs`. In interpreted code, `reflect.TypeOf` on an `embed.FS` value would report that path, not `embed`. This
might matter if interpreted code uses reflection to get the package path. But this is a minor edge case and not in the requirements.

Another potential issue: if a script imports both `embed` and `io/fs`, and uses `fs.FS` interface, the `embedfs.FS` implements it (verified). Good.

Let me check the test progress.

commit 7bb73753bf562338324afd27e01a35db78c436ba
Author: Yaegi Dev <dev@yaegi.local>
Date:   Thu Aug 6 05:18:36 2026 +0000

    interp: support //go:embed directives
    
    Implement //go:embed support for package-level variables of type string,
    []byte and embed.FS. Patterns are resolved relative to the source file
    directory using the interpreter source filesystem, with path.Match glob
    semantics, directory tree embedding, and hidden file exclusion unless the
    all: prefix is used. Embedded values are initialized before the first
    interpreted statement executes and are not overwritten by standard
    variable initialization.
    
    The embed.FS target is backed by a new internal read-only filesystem that
    implements fs.FS, fs.ReadFileFS and fs.ReadDirFS, with sorted ReadDir
    entries, fs.ReadDirFile directories and independent ReadFile copies. The
    embed package is registered through stdlib.Symbols.
    
    Add unit tests, an integration test in _test, and documentation.

Summary:
1. Added `//go:embed` directive detection in `interp/ast.go` by parsing comments (now always enabled), extracting patterns from `GenDecl.Doc` and
`ValueSpec.Doc` for standalone and grouped var declarations.
2. Added `interp/embed.go` with pattern parsing, glob resolution against the interpreter source filesystem, hidden-file exclusion with `all:` support,
and the `genEmbedVar` initializer that sets the embedded value without standard init overwriting it.
3. Added compile-time validation in `interp/cfg.go` for string/[]byte exactly-one-file and valid target types, and overrides global var generation for
embedded vars.

`fs.ReadDirFile` directories, and independent `ReadFile` copies.
5. Registered the `embed` package in `stdlib/embed.go` exposing `embed.FS` as the interpreter's embed filesystem type.

MESSAGE
I've completed the implementation and verified it thoroughly. Let me summarize what was done:

## Summary

**Feature: `//go:embed` directive support in the yaegi Go interpreter**

### Implementation
1. **`interp/ast.go`** — Parse comments in all modes and detect `//go:embed` directives from `ast.GenDecl.Doc` (standalone `var`) and
`ast.ValueSpec.Doc` (grouped `var (...)`), with validation for package-level scope, single variable, and no initializer.

2. **`interp/embed.go`** (new) — Pattern parsing (including quoted patterns), glob resolution using `path.Match` against the interpreter source
filesystem, directory-tree embedding, `.`/`_` hidden-file exclusion with `all:` support, and the `genEmbedVar` initializer that sets embedded content
without standard variable initialization overwriting it.

3. **`interp/cfg.go`** — Compile-time type validation (string/`[]byte` must match exactly one file; valid target types only) and generation override
for embedded globals.

4. **`internal/embedfs/embedfs.go`** (new) — A read-only in-memory filesystem implementing `fs.FS`, `fs.ReadFileFS`, and `fs.ReadDirFS`, with sorted
`ReadDir` entries, directories implementing `fs.ReadDirFile`, and independent `ReadFile` copies per call.

5. **`stdlib/embed.go`** (new) — Registers the `embed` package (`embed.FS`) via `stdlib.Symbols` so interpreted code can import `embed`.

6. **Tests & docs** — Comprehensive unit tests (`interp/embed_test.go`), a real-filesystem integration test (`_test/embed.go` +
`_test/embeddata.txt`), and documentation in `interp/doc.go`.

### Verification
- All 24 embed-specific tests pass.
- Full `go test ./interp` passes (with proper GOPATH): `ok ... 207.215s`.
- Other packages (`stdlib`, `cmd`, `extract`, `internal`) pass.
- `go vet` and `gofmt` clean.

All changes are committed on the `feature/embed-directive` branch.
